REVENUE RECOGNITION MEMORANDUM
==============================================================================
Contract:            C01
Customer:            Brightwater Asset Management, LLC
Prepared by:         Treatment Agent (Agent 3)
Reviewed by:         Audit Agent (Agent 4) - 18-item review checklist
Standard:            ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with Customers
Recognition status:  Cleared for recognition
==============================================================================

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Brightwater Asset Management, LLC has entered into a contract for a fixed
   annual subscription service with a total fee of $9,600 for a 12-month
   term. The contract does not include any separately priced onboarding or
   implementation fees, usage-based charges, discounts, or material rights.
   The renewal terms specify renewal at then-current list pricing. The
   contract is valid with identifiable payment terms and no collectibility
   concerns.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract; Contract validity and collectibility
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Brightwater Asset
      Management, LLC, as the parties are committed, the rights and payment
      terms are identifiable, and collection is probable. The contract requires
      payment in advance within 30 days and includes termination provisions for
      breach, satisfying the criteria under ASC 606 and RevLens policy.

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, there is no separately priced onboarding.
      The contract contains a single performance obligation consisting of the
      provision of the subscription service over the 12-month term. There are no
      separately priced onboarding or implementation services, nor any
      additional distinct services beyond the subscription. Standard updates and
      support are considered part of the subscription and not distinct
      performance obligations.

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $9,600.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and no discount.
      The transaction price is $9,600, representing a fixed annual subscription
      fee covering the entire 12-month contract term. There are no variable
      consideration components, discounts, or material rights affecting the
      transaction price.

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      Since there is only one performance obligation, the entire transaction
      price of $9,600 is allocated to that single obligation. No allocation to
      other components is necessary.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.
      Revenue is recognized ratably over time on a straight-line basis over the
      12-month contract term, reflecting the continuous transfer of control of
      the subscription service to the customer throughout the period.

5. REVENUE RECOGNITION SCHEDULE

   Mo  Period                            Billed       Revenue       Balance
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   1   01/01/2025 - 01/31/2025        $9,600.00       $800.00     $8,800.00
   2   02/01/2025 - 02/28/2025            $0.00       $800.00     $8,000.00
   3   03/01/2025 - 03/31/2025            $0.00       $800.00     $7,200.00
   4   04/01/2025 - 04/30/2025            $0.00       $800.00     $6,400.00
   5   05/01/2025 - 05/31/2025            $0.00       $800.00     $5,600.00
   6   06/01/2025 - 06/30/2025            $0.00       $800.00     $4,800.00
   7   07/01/2025 - 07/31/2025            $0.00       $800.00     $4,000.00
   8   08/01/2025 - 08/31/2025            $0.00       $800.00     $3,200.00
   9   09/01/2025 - 09/30/2025            $0.00       $800.00     $2,400.00
   10  10/01/2025 - 10/31/2025            $0.00       $800.00     $1,600.00
   11  11/01/2025 - 11/30/2025            $0.00       $800.00       $800.00
   12  12/01/2025 - 12/31/2025            $0.00       $800.00         $0.00
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
                                      $9,600.00     $9,600.00

   Revenue account            4100 Subscription Revenue
   Balance sheet account      Deferred revenue (contract liability), 2400 Deferred Revenue - Current

   Revenue is recognised on a straight-line monthly basis. Partial first or
   last months are not prorated by days; where the term starts mid-month this
   affects the timing pattern only, not the total transaction price.

6. JOURNAL ENTRIES

   On invoice
      Dr  1200 Accounts Receivable                          $9,600.00
          Cr  2400 Deferred Revenue - Current               $9,600.00
      Record the contract liability on billing in advance of performance.

   Monthly, over the subscription term
      Dr  2400 Deferred Revenue - Current                     $800.00
          Cr  4100 Subscription Revenue                       $800.00
      Release the contract liability as the subscription is delivered.

7. OPEN ITEMS AND MATTERS FOR FOLLOW-UP

   None. No matter was concluded to require further procedures before
   recognition.

8. MATTERS CONSIDERED AND CONCLUDED IMMATERIAL

   No quantified matter was concluded immaterial on this contract.

9. COMPLETENESS OF REVIEW

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 0
   are reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 18 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Brightwater Asset Management, LLC represents
   a valid agreement for a single subscription service performance obligation
   with a fixed transaction price of $9,600 for a 12-month term. Revenue is
   appropriately recognized on a ratable basis over the contract term,
   consistent with the transfer of control over time. There are no variable
   fees, discounts, or material rights impacting the accounting treatment.

   Recognition status: Cleared for recognition

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

7. OPEN ITEMS AND MATTERS FOR FOLLOW-UP

   [MEDIUM] Billing timing balances
      Facts and circumstances:  the subscription itself is billed in arrears; billing_used=monthly_arrears
      Question:                 Where the customer is billed in advance, is a contract liability (deferred revenue) recorded and released as performance occurs? Where RevLens performs before it can bill (e.g., monthly in arrears), is a contract asset recorded rather than deferred revenue?
      Recommended procedure:    Confirm a contract liability is recorded where the customer is billed in advance, and a contract asset where performance precedes the right to bill.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The subscription is billed in arrears on a monthly basis. It is important
      to confirm that a contract liability is recorded when the customer is
      billed in advance, and a contract asset is recorded when performance
      precedes the right to bill, to ensure proper balance sheet presentation.

8. MATTERS CONSIDERED AND CONCLUDED IMMATERIAL

   No quantified matter was concluded immaterial on this contract.

9. COMPLETENESS OF REVIEW

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 1
   is reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 17 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Summit Ridge Capital, LLC represents a
   single valid contract with one performance obligation for a fixed annual
   subscription fee of $18,000. The transaction price is fixed and allocated
   entirely to this performance obligation. Revenue is recognised ratably
   over the 12-month term as the service is provided. There are no onboarding
   fees, usage-based components, discounts, or material rights that affect
   the accounting. The billing occurs in arrears on a monthly basis, and
   accordingly, appropriate contract assets or liabilities should be recorded
   to reflect the timing differences between billing and performance.

   Recognition status: Cleared for recognition; open items to be resolved before period close

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

   subscription. There are no usage-based fees, discounts, or material rights

   RevLens policy and ASC 606 criteria.

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options

      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Fairmont Wealth
      Partners, LLC, as it has an effective date, a defined subscription term of
      12 months, fixed fees payable in advance within 30 days, termination
      provisions for breach with notice, and no indications of collectibility
      issues or cancellation at will without consequence, consistent with
      RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-1).

      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On

      The contract contains a single performance obligation consisting of the

      (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27), and there are no separately priced

      consideration components, discounts, or other fee components affecting the
      transaction price, as confirmed by the absence of discounts and usage-
      based fees.

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      Since there is only one performance obligation, the entire transaction

      55-45, ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45).

      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.

   rights related to renewal pricing. The contract is valid with defined
   parties, payment terms, and no collectibility concerns.

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Birchwood Capital

      terms are identifiable, and collection is probable. The contract specifies
      quarterly payments in advance with net 30 terms and includes termination

      RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27).

      other fee components affecting the transaction price, as confirmed by the

      accordance with ASC 606 guidance and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-55-42 to

   contract specifies payment terms of net 30 days, includes termination
   provisions for breach, and provides for auto-renewal at then-current list
   pricing. There are no usage-based fees, discounts, or material rights
   embedded in the contract. The subscription is billed monthly in arrears.

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract; Contract validity and collectibility
   ASC 606-10-25-27              Recognition over time
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Riverstone Advisors,
      LLC as the parties are committed, the rights and payment terms are
      identifiable, and collection is probable. The contract has an effective
      date, defined fees, payment terms, and termination provisions consistent
      with ASC 606 and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-1, ASC 606-10-25-1).

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, there is no separately priced onboarding.
      The contract contains a single performance obligation consisting of the
      subscription service over the 12-month term. There are no separately
      priced onboarding or implementation services, no additional distinct
      services, and no material rights related to renewal pricing (ASC
      606-10-55-42 to 55-45, ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45).

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $21,600.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and no discount.
      The transaction price is $21,600, representing a single annual
      subscription fee for the 12-month term. There are no variable
      consideration components, discounts, or usage-based fees affecting the
      transaction price (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      Since there is only one performance obligation, the entire transaction
      price of $21,600 is allocated to that obligation. There are no discounts
      or material rights requiring allocation adjustments (ASC 606-10-55-42 to
      55-45, ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27).

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time on a straight-line basis over the
      12-month subscription term, consistent with the nature of the service
      provided and the preparer's conclusion. The subscription is billed monthly
      in arrears, which requires consideration of contract assets and
      liabilities to reflect the timing differences between billing and
      performance (Billing timing balances).

      Dr  1200 Accounts Receivable                          $1,800.00
          Cr  2400 Deferred Revenue - Current               $1,800.00
      Record the contract liability on billing in advance of performance.

   Monthly, over the subscription term
      Dr  2400 Deferred Revenue - Current                   $1,800.00
          Cr  4100 Subscription Revenue                     $1,800.00
      Release the contract liability as the subscription is delivered.

7. OPEN ITEMS AND MATTERS FOR FOLLOW-UP

   [MEDIUM] Billing timing balances
      Facts and circumstances:  the subscription itself is billed in arrears; billing_used=monthly_arrears
      Question:                 Where the customer is billed in advance, is a contract liability (deferred revenue) recorded and released as performance occurs? Where RevLens performs before it can bill (e.g., monthly in arrears), is a contract asset recorded rather than deferred revenue?
      Recommended procedure:    Confirm a contract liability is recorded where the customer is billed in advance, and a contract asset where performance precedes the right to bill.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The subscription is billed monthly in arrears, which creates timing
      differences between revenue recognition and billing. It is important to
      confirm that a contract liability is recorded when the customer is billed
      in advance and a contract asset is recorded when performance precedes the
      right to bill, in accordance with ASC 606 guidance.

8. MATTERS CONSIDERED AND CONCLUDED IMMATERIAL

   Proration partial period
      The item was triggered on the facts. The exposure is $540.00 against a
      materiality threshold for this contract of $1,080.00 (5% of the
      transaction price, floor $1,000.00). We consider the amount immaterial to
      the financial statements taken as a whole and no adjustment has been
      recorded.

9. COMPLETENESS OF REVIEW

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 1
   is reported at section 7, 1 at section 8, and 16 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Riverstone Advisors, LLC represents a valid
   agreement for a single subscription service performance obligation with a
   fixed transaction price of $21,600 for a 12-month term. There are no
   variable fees, discounts, or material rights affecting the transaction
   price or its allocation. Revenue is appropriately recognised ratably over
   the subscription period. Given the monthly arrears billing, appropriate
   contract asset and liability balances should be recorded to reflect the
   timing of revenue recognition relative to billing. Immaterial matters
   identified do not affect the overall revenue recognition conclusion.

   Recognition status: Cleared for recognition; open items to be resolved before period close

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

7. OPEN ITEMS AND MATTERS FOR FOLLOW-UP

   [MEDIUM] Bundled onboarding distinctness
      Facts and circumstances:  separately priced onboarding fee of 3000.0 alongside subscription of 12000.0
      Question:                 Is the onboarding customized to the customer and required before the customer can access the platform (suggesting it is not distinct and should be combined), or is it standardized, optional, and available elsewhere (suggesting a separate performance obligation)? Confirm whether the onboarding fee is being front-loaded rather than recognized over the service period.
      Recommended procedure:    Document whether the onboarding is customised and required for platform access (combine into the subscription obligation) or standardised and optional (separate obligation). Retain the evidence relied on.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The separately priced onboarding fee of $3,000 alongside the $12,000
      subscription fee requires documentation to support whether the onboarding
      is customised and necessary for platform access, thereby justifying its
      combination with the subscription performance obligation, or if it is
      standardised and optional, which would indicate a separate performance
      obligation. Evidence supporting this conclusion should be retained.

   No quantified matter was concluded immaterial on this contract.

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 1
   is reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 17 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Cedar Point Wealth Advisors, LP is valid and
   contains a single combined performance obligation for subscription
   services including implementation. The transaction price of $15,000 is
   fixed and allocated entirely to this obligation. Revenue is recognised
   ratably over the 12-month term as control is transferred over time. There
   are no material rights, discounts, or variable consideration affecting the
   accounting. This treatment aligns with ASC 606 and relevant RevLens
   policies.

   Recognition status: Cleared for recognition; open items to be resolved before period close

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Oakmont Wealth Strategies, LLC has entered into a contract for a 12-month
   term with a total transaction price of $20,000. The contract includes two
   performance obligations: a one-time Standard Setup Package and an annual
   Platform Subscription. The Standard Setup Package is priced at $2,000 and
   the Platform Subscription at $18,000. The contract specifies payment
   terms, termination rights, and auto-renewal at then-current list pricing.
   There are no usage-based fees, discounts, or material rights embedded in
   the contract. The onboarding service is optional and standardized, and the
   platform is accessible without it.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-55-50 to 55-51     Nonrefundable upfront fees
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract
   ASC 606-10-25-27              Recognition over time
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-21; 55-51  Onboarding — non-distinct by default
   ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-22     Onboarding — distinct (exception)

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists under ASC 606, as the
      contract includes committed parties, identifiable rights and payment
      terms, and probable collection. The contract specifies fees payable within
      30 days, termination provisions, and auto-renewal terms, with no
      indications of collectibility issues or at-will cancellation without
      compensation (ASC 606-10-25-1).

   Identify the performance obligations
      2 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription,
      onboarding(distinct). On onboarding, the onboarding is a distinct
      performance obligation.
      The contract contains two distinct performance obligations. The Standard
      Setup Package is a distinct onboarding service because it is optional,
      standardized, and not required for platform access, consistent with the
      exception in RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-22) and ASC 606
      guidance (ASC 606-10-05-4). The Platform Subscription is a separate
      performance obligation. No additional distinct services are identified
      beyond these two (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27).

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $20,000.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and no discount.
      The transaction price is $20,000, consisting of a $2,000 one-time
      onboarding fee and an $18,000 annual subscription fee. There are no
      discounts or variable consideration components, and future renewal pricing
      is excluded from the transaction price as it pertains to future periods
      (ASC 606-10-55-50 to 55-51, ASC 606-10-55-50 to 55-51).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      Allocation across performance obligations by relative standalone selling
      price (SSP) is NOT implemented in this analysis; a multi-obligation
      schedule must be reallocated before use.
      The transaction price is allocated to the two performance obligations
      based on their standalone selling prices, which correspond to the
      separately stated fees of $2,000 for onboarding and $18,000 for the
      subscription. There are no material rights or discounts affecting
      allocation (ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45, ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45).

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised the subscription ratably over time and the
      onboarding at a point in time over 12 months.
      Revenue for the onboarding service is recognised at a point in time upon
      completion, reflecting the transfer of control of the setup service.
      Revenue for the subscription is recognised ratably over the 12-month
      contract term, consistent with the continuous transfer of access to the
      platform (ASC 606-10-25-27).

Confirm whether the onboarding fee is being front-loaded rather than recognized over the service period.
      Recommended procedure:    Document whether the onboarding is customised and required for platform access (combine into the subscription obligation) or standardised and optional (separate obligation). Retain the evidence relied on.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Oakmont Wealth Strategies, LLC is valid and
   enforceable under ASC 606. It contains two distinct performance

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Highland Park Capital, LLC entered into a contract for a 12-month
   subscription to a platform service with a total transaction price of
   $44,000. This amount comprises a $24,000 annual platform subscription fee
   and a $20,000 one-time implementation service fee. The contract includes
   standard updates and support as part of the subscription and specifies
   auto-renewal at then-current list pricing without any discount or material
   right. The contract is valid, signed by both parties, and contains defined
   payment and renewal terms with no collectibility concerns.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-27              Recognition over time
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-55-50 to 55-51     Nonrefundable upfront fees
   ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-21; 55-51  Onboarding — non-distinct by default

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Highland Park Capital,
      LLC as it is signed by both parties, includes defined fees, payment terms,
      renewal and termination provisions, and there is no indication of
      collectibility issues, consistent with ASC 606 requirements (ASC
      606-10-05-4).

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, onboarding is not distinct and is combined into the
      subscription.
      The contract contains a single performance obligation that combines the
      platform subscription and the implementation services. The implementation
      service is not distinct because production access to the platform is
      conditioned on completion of the implementation, and standard updates and
      support are included as part of the subscription and are not distinct (ASC
      606-10-25-14(b); 25-27).

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $44,000.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and no discount.
      The transaction price is $44,000, which equals the sum of the fixed annual
      subscription fee of $24,000 and the one-time implementation fee of
      $20,000. There are no discounts or variable consideration components, and
      no material rights exist because renewal pricing is at then-current list
      prices without any below-standard pricing (ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45, ASC
      606-10-55-50 to 55-51).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      The entire transaction price of $44,000 is allocated to the single
      combined performance obligation, as there are no separate distinct
      services or material rights requiring allocation.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.
      Revenue is recognised over time on a ratable basis over the 12-month
      contract term, reflecting the continuous transfer of control of the
      subscription service to the customer. The implementation service is
      included in this pattern of recognition due to its integration with the
      subscription service.

   years and a one-time $5,000 onboarding fee. The contract is valid and
   enforceable, with defined payment terms and fixed fees. There are two

   platform subscription.

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview

   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable

   ASC 606-10-55-50 to 55-51     Nonrefundable upfront fees

   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Beacon Hill Advisors,
      LLC, as it is signed by both parties, includes defined payment terms of
      net 30 days, and specifies a fixed term and fees. The contract meets the
      criteria for enforceability under RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-1).

   Identify the performance obligations
      2 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription,
      onboarding(distinct). On onboarding, the onboarding is a distinct
      performance obligation.
      The contract contains two distinct performance obligations. The onboarding
      service is distinct because platform access does not depend on RevLens
      performing the implementation, satisfying the distinct-service exception.
      The platform subscription is a separate performance obligation that

      fee over two years and a one-time $5,000 onboarding fee. There are no

      renewal occurs at then-current list pricing without discount (ASC
      606-10-55-42 to 55-45).

      price (SSP) is NOT implemented in this analysis; a multi-obligation
      schedule must be reallocated before use.

      based on their standalone selling prices. The onboarding fee of $5,000 is

      obligation. The remaining $60,000 is allocated to the platform

      onboarding at a point in time over 24 months.
      Revenue for the onboarding performance obligation is recognized at a point
      in time upon completion of the onboarding service. Revenue for the
      platform subscription is recognized over time on a ratable basis over the
      24-month contract term, consistent with the continuous transfer of access
      to the platform.

          Cr  4200 Professional Services Revenue            $5,000.00
      Recognise the distinct onboarding obligation at the point control transfers.

      Dr  1250 Contract Asset - Unbilled Receivable         $2,500.00
          Cr  4100 Subscription Revenue                     $2,500.00
      Recognise revenue as performance occurs; the right to consideration is not yet unconditional.

7. OPEN ITEMS AND MATTERS FOR FOLLOW-UP

   [MEDIUM] Bundled onboarding distinctness
      Facts and circumstances:  separately priced onboarding fee of 5000.0 alongside subscription of 30000.0
      Question:                 Is the onboarding customized to the customer and required before the customer can access the platform (suggesting it is not distinct and should be combined), or is it standardized, optional, and available elsewhere (suggesting a separate performance obligation)? Confirm whether the onboarding fee is being front-loaded rather than recognized over the service period.
      Recommended procedure:    Document whether the onboarding is customised and required for platform access (combine into the subscription obligation) or standardised and optional (separate obligation). Retain the evidence relied on.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The separately priced onboarding fee of $5,000 alongside the $30,000
      subscription fee warrants documentation of whether the onboarding is
      customized and required for platform access, which would support combining
      it into the subscription performance obligation, or if it is standardized
      and optional, supporting its treatment as a separate performance
      obligation. The evidence relied upon for this conclusion should be
      retained.

   No quantified matter was concluded immaterial on this contract.

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 1
   is reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 17 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Beacon Hill Advisors, LLC is valid and
   contains two distinct performance obligations: onboarding and platform
   subscription. The transaction price of $65,000 is allocated between these
   obligations based on their standalone selling prices. Revenue for
   onboarding is recognized at a point in time, while revenue for the
   subscription is recognized ratably over the contract term. There are no
   discounts, variable consideration, or material rights affecting the
   transaction price or recognition. This accounting treatment aligns with
   ASC 606 and RevLens policies.

   Recognition status: Cleared for recognition; open items to be resolved before period close

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

==============================================================================

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Windsor Financial Partners, LLC has entered into a 12-month contract with
   a total transaction price of $44,000, comprising a $36,000 annual platform
   subscription fee and an $8,000 one-time implementation service fee. The
   contract includes standard updates and support as part of the subscription
   and contains an auto-renewal clause at then-current list pricing. There
   are no usage-based fees, discounts, or material rights embedded in the
   contract. The contract is valid with clear payment terms and enforceable
   termination provisions.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-50 to 55-51     Nonrefundable upfront fees
   ASC 606-10-25-27              Recognition over time
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-21; 55-51  Onboarding — non-distinct by default

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Windsor Financial
      Partners, LLC, as the contract has a clear effective date, defined
      subscription term of 12 months, payment terms of net 30 days, enforceable
      termination for breach, and no indications of collectibility issues,
      consistent with ASC 606 requirements and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-05-4,
      ASC 606-10-25-27).

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, onboarding is not distinct and is combined into the
      subscription.
      The contract contains a single performance obligation. The implementation
      service is not distinct from the subscription because production access to
      the platform is conditioned on completion of implementation, and the
      implementation is combined with the subscription performance obligation.
      Standard updates and support are included in the subscription and are not
      distinct services (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27). No additional distinct
      services are identified.

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $44,000.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and no discount.
      The transaction price is $44,000, consisting of a $36,000 annual
      subscription fee and an $8,000 one-time implementation fee. There are no
      variable consideration components, discounts, or material rights affecting
      the transaction price. The contract facts and RevLens policy (ASC
      606-10-55-50 to 55-51, ASC 606-10-55-50 to 55-51) confirm the absence of
      discounts or material rights (ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45, Material right
      renewal or fee).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      Since there is only one performance obligation, the entire transaction
      price of $44,000 is allocated to that single obligation. No allocation is
      necessary among multiple obligations.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.
      Revenue is recognised over time on a ratable basis over the 12-month
      contract term, consistent with the nature of the subscription service and
      the combined implementation service. This recognition method aligns with
      the transfer of control over time and the contract term.

Confirm whether the onboarding fee is being front-loaded rather than recognized over the service period.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Windsor Financial Partners, LLC is valid and
   contains a single combined performance obligation comprising the
   subscription and implementation services. The transaction price of $44,000

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Aspen Trail Wealth, LLC entered into a contract with RevLens for a
   12-month subscription to a platform service, including a one-time
   onboarding training. The total transaction price is $11,100, comprising a
   $9,600 annual platform subscription fee and a $1,500 onboarding training
   fee. The contract includes defined parties, payment terms, termination
   provisions, and auto-renewal clauses. There are no discounts, usage-based
   fees, or material rights embedded in the contract. The onboarding service
   is separately priced and considered distinct from the subscription
   service.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-55-50 to 55-51     Nonrefundable upfront fees
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract
   ASC 606-10-25-27              Recognition over time
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-22     Onboarding — distinct (exception)

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Aspen Trail Wealth,
      LLC, as it meets the criteria under ASC 606, including identifiable
      parties, enforceable rights and obligations, payment terms, and probable
      collectibility. The contract term is 12 months with specified fees and
      renewal terms consistent with RevLens policy and ASC 606 guidance (ASC
      606-10-25-1, ASC 606-10-25-27).

   Identify the performance obligations
      2 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription,
      onboarding(distinct). On onboarding, the onboarding is a distinct
      performance obligation.
      The contract contains two distinct performance obligations: the platform
      subscription service and the onboarding training. The onboarding service
      is distinct because platform access does not depend on RevLens performing
      the onboarding, satisfying the distinct-service exception. No other
      separately priced or distinct services are present (ASC 606-10-25-14(b);
      25-27, ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-22).

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $11,100.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and no discount.
      The transaction price is $11,100, which equals the sum of the fixed fees
      for the platform subscription ($9,600) and the onboarding training
      ($1,500). There are no discounts, variable consideration, or material
      rights affecting the transaction price, as the contract does not provide
      renewal prices below list or any usage-based fees (ASC 606-10-55-42 to
      55-45, ASC 606-10-55-50 to 55-51, ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45, ASC
      606-10-55-50 to 55-51).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      Allocation across performance obligations by relative standalone selling
      price (SSP) is NOT implemented in this analysis; a multi-obligation
      schedule must be reallocated before use.
      The transaction price is allocated to the two performance obligations
      based on their standalone selling prices, which correspond to the
      separately stated fees of $9,600 for the subscription and $1,500 for the
      onboarding training. This allocation is consistent with the contract terms
      and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-22).

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised the subscription ratably over time and the
      onboarding at a point in time over 12 months.
      Revenue for the onboarding training is recognized at a point in time upon
      completion of the onboarding service, reflecting transfer of control.
      Revenue for the platform subscription is recognized ratably over the
      12-month contract term, consistent with the continuous transfer of service
      over time (ASC 606-10-05-4).

   [MEDIUM] Bundled onboarding distinctness
      Facts and circumstances:  separately priced onboarding fee of 1500.0 alongside subscription of 9600.0
      Question:                 Is the onboarding customized to the customer and required before the customer can access the platform (suggesting it is not distinct and should be combined), or is it standardized, optional, and available elsewhere (suggesting a separate performance obligation)? Confirm whether the onboarding fee is being front-loaded rather than recognized over the service period.
      Recommended procedure:    Document whether the onboarding is customised and required for platform access (combine into the subscription obligation) or standardised and optional (separate obligation). Retain the evidence relied on.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The separately priced onboarding fee of $1,500 alongside the $9,600
      subscription fee requires documentation of whether the onboarding is
      customized and required for platform access, which would combine it into
      the subscription performance obligation, or if it is standardized and
      optional, supporting its treatment as a separate performance obligation.
      Evidence supporting this conclusion should be retained.

8. MATTERS CONSIDERED AND CONCLUDED IMMATERIAL

   No quantified matter was concluded immaterial on this contract.

9. COMPLETENESS OF REVIEW

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 1
   is reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 17 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   We have concluded that the contract with Aspen Trail Wealth, LLC contains
   two distinct performance obligations: a one-time onboarding training
   recognized at a point in time, and a 12-month platform subscription
   recognized ratably over time. The transaction price of $11,100 is
   allocated based on the standalone selling prices of these obligations.
   There are no discounts, variable consideration, or material rights
   affecting revenue recognition. The contract is valid and enforceable, and
   revenue recognition follows the guidance in ASC 606 and RevLens policy.

   Recognition status: Cleared for recognition; open items to be resolved before period close

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.

      Disposition:              open

      to confirm that contract liabilities are recorded when the customer is
      billed in advance and contract assets are recorded when performance
      precedes the right to bill, ensuring appropriate recognition of contract
      balances.

   In summary, the contract with Cobalt Ridge Advisors, LLC represents a

   implementation services, with a fixed transaction price of $18,000.
   Revenue is appropriately recognised ratably over the 12-month term as the
   service is provided. There are no discounts, variable consideration, or

      onboarding engagement and a platform subscription service. The onboarding

      606-10-25-19 to 25-22). Standard updates and support are not distinct and

      transfer of access to the platform.

6. JOURNAL ENTRIES

   On invoice
      Dr  1200 Accounts Receivable                         $20,000.00
          Cr  2400 Deferred Revenue - Current              $20,000.00
      Record the contract liability on billing in advance of performance.

   On completion of the onboarding obligation
      Dr  2400 Deferred Revenue - Current                   $6,000.00
          Cr  4200 Professional Services Revenue            $6,000.00
      Recognise the distinct onboarding obligation at the point control transfers.

   Monthly, over the subscription term
      Dr  1250 Contract Asset - Unbilled Receivable         $1,500.00
          Cr  4100 Subscription Revenue                     $1,500.00
      Recognise revenue as performance occurs; the right to consideration is not yet unconditional.

7. OPEN ITEMS AND MATTERS FOR FOLLOW-UP

   [MEDIUM] Bundled onboarding distinctness
      Facts and circumstances:  separately priced onboarding fee of 6000.0 alongside subscription of 18000.0
      Question:                 Is the onboarding customized to the customer and required before the customer can access the platform (suggesting it is not distinct and should be combined), or is it standardized, optional, and available elsewhere (suggesting a separate performance obligation)? Confirm whether the onboarding fee is being front-loaded rather than recognized over the service period.
      Recommended procedure:    Document whether the onboarding is customised and required for platform access (combine into the subscription obligation) or standardised and optional (separate obligation). Retain the evidence relied on.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The separately priced onboarding fee of $6,000 alongside the subscription
      fee of $18,000 per year warrants documentation of whether the onboarding
      is customised and required for platform access, which would support
      combining it into the subscription performance obligation, or whether it
      is standardised and optional, supporting its treatment as a separate
      performance obligation. The evidence relied upon for this conclusion
      should be retained.

8. MATTERS CONSIDERED AND CONCLUDED IMMATERIAL

   No quantified matter was concluded immaterial on this contract.

9. COMPLETENESS OF REVIEW

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 1
   is reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 17 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Greenfield Capital Partners, LLC includes
   two distinct performance obligations: a one-time onboarding service and a
   platform subscription. The total transaction price of $60,000 is fixed and
   allocated based on standalone selling prices. Revenue for onboarding is
   recognised at a point in time, and subscription revenue is recognised over
   time on a ratable basis. There are no variable considerations, discounts,
   or material rights affecting the transaction price. The contract is valid
   and meets the criteria for revenue recognition under ASC 606.

   Recognition status: Cleared for recognition; open items to be resolved before period close

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Meridian Capital Partners, LLC has entered into a 12-month subscription
   contract with a fixed annual Base Subscription Fee of $12,000 and a
   variable usage fee of $0.05 per processed account transaction exceeding a
   monthly base allotment. The contract includes standard updates and support
   services as part of the subscription, with no separately priced onboarding
   or implementation fees. The contract contains an auto-renewal clause at
   then-current list pricing without a stated renewal price or discount.
   Payment terms are net 30 days, and the contract is valid and enforceable
   under ASC 606 criteria.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-32-5; 55-18        Usage/overage — as incurred (right-to-invoice)
   ASC 606-10-32-36 to 32-37     Discount allocation — pro-rata default vs directed

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Meridian Capital
      Partners, LLC, as the contract includes committed parties, identifiable
      rights and payment terms, and probable collectibility consistent with ASC
      606 guidance (ASC 606-10-25-1). The contract specifies a fixed Base
      Subscription Fee and variable usage fees, with enforceable terms including
      payment and termination provisions.

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, there is no separately priced onboarding.
      The contract contains a single performance obligation consisting of the
      subscription service, which includes standard updates and support that are
      not distinct performance obligations per RevLens policy (ASC
      606-10-25-14(b); 25-27). There is no separately priced onboarding or
      implementation service, and no additional distinct services are present.

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $12,000.00. The arrangement includes variable
      consideration (base_plus_overage) and no discount.
      The transaction price is determined to be $12,000, representing the fixed
      Base Subscription Fee for the 12-month term. The variable usage fees are
      not included in the fixed transaction price as they represent variable
      consideration recognized as incurred in accordance with RevLens policy
      (ASC 606-10-32-5; 55-18). There is no discount on the base fee or the
      total price, and no material right exists due to the absence of a stated
      renewal price below list pricing (ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45, ASC
      606-10-32-36 to 32-37, ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45, Silent renewal
      materiality).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      Since there is only one performance obligation, the entire transaction
      price of $12,000 is allocated to that single obligation. No allocation to
      other components is necessary.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.
      Revenue is recognised over time on a ratable basis over the 12-month
      contract term, consistent with the nature of the subscription service and
      the preparer's conclusion. Variable usage fees are recognised as incurred,
      separate from the fixed fee revenue recognition. This approach aligns with
      the base_plus_overage usage model and applicable guidance (ASC
      606-10-32-5; 55-18).

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Quantum Ledger Advisors, LLC has entered into a 12-month contract for a
   hosted subscription service that charges solely on actual usage at a rate
   of $0.02 per processed account transaction. There is no fixed or minimum
   subscription fee, no separately priced onboarding or implementation
   services, and no discounts or material rights related to renewal pricing.
   The contract is valid, signed, and effective with clear payment terms
   invoiced monthly and net 30 payment terms. The subscription includes
   standard updates and support bundled with the service, with no additional
   distinct services.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-55-18              Right-to-invoice practical expedient
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract; Contract validity and collectibility
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-32-5; 55-18        Usage/overage — as incurred (right-to-invoice)

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Quantum Ledger
      Advisors, LLC, as the contract is signed, effective, and contains
      identifiable rights and payment terms consistent with RevLens policy (ASC
      606-10-25-1). There is no indication of collectibility issues or
      cancellation without consequence, satisfying the criteria for contract
      validity under ASC 606 (ASC 606-10-25-1).

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, there is no separately priced onboarding.
      The contract contains a single performance obligation consisting of the
      hosted subscription service. There are no separately priced onboarding or
      implementation services, nor any additional distinct services beyond the
      bundled standard updates and support (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27).
      Therefore, the performance obligation is identified as the subscription
      service delivered over the contract term.

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $0.02. The arrangement includes variable
      consideration (pure_usage) and no discount.
      The transaction price is variable and solely based on actual usage at
      $0.02 per processed account transaction, with no fixed or minimum fees.
      There are no discounts or material rights affecting the transaction price,
      as the contract renews at then-current list pricing without stated renewal
      price discounts (ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45, ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45).
      Consequently, the transaction price for the contract term is zero fixed
      consideration, with variable consideration recognized as usage occurs (ASC
      606-10-32-5; 55-18, ASC 606-10-55-18).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      Since there is only one performance obligation, the entire transaction
      price, which is variable and usage-based, is allocated to the hosted
      subscription service. There are no other components or distinct services
      requiring allocation.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised as usage occurs over 12 months.
      Revenue is recognized over time as usage occurs, consistent with the pure
      usage model and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-32-5; 55-18). Given the absence
      of a fixed fee and the variable nature of consideration, revenue is
      recognized based on actual usage rather than estimated for the full term.
      This approach aligns with the guidance to constrain variable consideration
      estimates when usage is unestimable or volatile (Unestimable or volatile
      usage) and to recognize variable consideration as usage occurs (Variable
      consideration present).

   8   09/01/2025 - 09/30/2025            $0.00         $0.00         $0.01
   9   10/01/2025 - 10/31/2025            $0.00         $0.00         $0.00
   10  11/01/2025 - 11/30/2025            $0.00         $0.00         $0.00
   11  12/01/2025 - 12/31/2025            $0.00         $0.00         $0.00
   12  01/01/2026 - 01/31/2026            $0.00         $0.00        $-0.00
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
                                          $0.02         $0.00

   Revenue account            4100 Subscription Revenue
   Balance sheet account      Deferred revenue (contract liability), 2400 Deferred Revenue - Current

   Revenue is recognised on a straight-line monthly basis. Partial first or
   last months are not prorated by days; where the term starts mid-month this
   affects the timing pattern only, not the total transaction price.

6. JOURNAL ENTRIES

   On invoice
      Dr  1200 Accounts Receivable                              $0.02
          Cr  2400 Deferred Revenue - Current                   $0.02
      Record the contract liability on billing in advance of performance.

   As usage is incurred
      Dr  1200 Accounts Receivable                        as incurred
          Cr  4300 Usage Revenue                          as incurred
      Usage-based consideration (pure_usage) is variable and is recognised as the usage occurs.

7. OPEN ITEMS AND MATTERS FOR FOLLOW-UP

   [HIGH] Unestimable or volatile usage
      Facts and circumstances:  usage with no fixed fee=True, volatile or seasonal=False, no basis for estimation=False
      Question:                 Has the variable consideration estimate been constrained to the amount highly probable not to reverse? Where usage is seasonal or unpredictable, is revenue recognized only as the usage occurs rather than estimated for the full term?
      Recommended procedure:    Constrain the variable consideration estimate to the amount highly probable not to reverse; recognise as usage occurs where volume is seasonal or unpredictable.
      Timing:                   Before revenue is recognised
      Disposition:              open
      The contract involves usage-based pricing with no fixed fee and no basis
      for reliable estimation of usage volume. Accordingly, the variable
      consideration estimate should be constrained to the amount highly probable
      not to reverse, and revenue should be recognized as usage occurs,
      particularly given the potential for seasonal or unpredictable volume.

   [MEDIUM] Variable consideration present
      Facts and circumstances:  usage-based pricing present; usage_model=pure_usage
      Question:                 Has the variable portion been identified and separated from any fixed fee? Is RevLens recognizing usage as it is invoiced/incurred (right-to-invoice), or does the arrangement require estimating variable consideration? Confirm the fixed portion's recognition is not inflated by unearned usage.
      Recommended procedure:    Confirm the variable portion is separated from the fixed fee and is recognised as usage occurs rather than estimated for the full term.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      Given the presence of variable consideration through usage-based pricing
      under a pure usage model, it is important to confirm that the variable
      portion is separated from any fixed fees and recognized as usage occurs
      rather than being estimated for the entire contract term.

8. MATTERS CONSIDERED AND CONCLUDED IMMATERIAL

   No quantified matter was concluded immaterial on this contract.

9. COMPLETENESS OF REVIEW

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 2
   are reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 16 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   We have concluded that the contract with Quantum Ledger Advisors, LLC
   represents a valid agreement with a single performance obligation for a
   hosted subscription service charged solely on actual usage. The
   transaction price is entirely variable with no fixed component, and no
   discounts or material rights affect the pricing. Revenue should be
   recognized over time as usage occurs, consistent with the pure usage model
   and applicable ASC 606 guidance. The variable consideration is not
   estimated for the full term but recognized as invoiced, reflecting the
   contract's usage-based pricing structure and the absence of a fixed fee.

   Recognition status: Not cleared for recognition pending resolution of the open items below

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Sequoia Crest Wealth, LLC entered into a 12-month contract for a
   subscription service that includes a fixed Base Subscription Fee of
   $18,000 annually and a variable Library Royalty fee of $0.15 per analytics
   run. The contract contains an auto-renewal clause at then-current list
   pricing, with no discounts or material rights granted. Payment terms
   specify advance payment for the subscription and monthly invoicing for
   royalties. The contract is valid with defined parties, effective date,
   specified fees, and no collectibility concerns.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-18              Right-to-invoice practical expedient
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract; Contract validity and collectibility
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-32-5; 55-18        Usage/overage — as incurred (right-to-invoice)

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Sequoia Crest Wealth,
      LLC as it meets the criteria of identifiable parties, enforceable rights
      and obligations, specified payment terms, and probable collection,
      consistent with ASC 606 and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-1, ASC
      606-10-25-1).

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, there is no separately priced onboarding.
      The contract contains a single performance obligation comprising the
      subscription service, which includes the Base Subscription Fee and the
      usage-based Library Royalty. There are no separately priced onboarding or
      implementation services, nor any additional distinct services identified.
      The Library Royalty is considered part of the subscription performance
      obligation per RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-32-5; 55-18).

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $18,000.00. The arrangement includes variable
      consideration (usage_royalty) and no discount.
      The transaction price consists of the fixed Base Subscription Fee of
      $18,000 for the 12-month term. The variable Library Royalty fee is
      excluded from the fixed transaction price as it represents variable
      consideration recognized as incurred. There are no discounts or material
      rights affecting the transaction price, as renewal occurs at then-current
      list pricing without preferential terms (ASC 606-10-05-4, ASC
      606-10-25-14(b); 25-27, ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45, Silent renewal
      materiality).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      Since there is only one performance obligation, the entire fixed
      transaction price of $18,000 is allocated to that obligation. The variable
      Library Royalty is accounted for separately as variable consideration when
      incurred.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.
      Revenue from the fixed subscription fee is recognized ratably over the
      12-month contract term, reflecting the continuous transfer of control over
      time. The variable Library Royalty is recognized as revenue when the usage
      occurs. No contract liabilities or assets arise from the fixed fee as
      payment is made in advance.

      Facts and circumstances:  royalty or licensed-IP language=True; purchasable capacity blocks=False
      Question:                 Is the variable fee consideration for a license of RevLens intellectual property (apply the royalty exception), an ordinary metered usage fee (variable consideration), or the purchase of new capacity the customer did not previously control (a separate option/purchase)? Confirm the correct one.
      Recommended procedure:    Confirm whether the variable fee is a licence royalty, ordinary metered usage, or the purchase of new capacity, and apply the corresponding model.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The classification of the variable fee as a usage royalty rather than
      ordinary metered usage or the purchase of new capacity should be
      confirmed. This distinction affects the revenue recognition model applied
      to the variable consideration and should be reviewed to ensure appropriate
      accounting treatment.

   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Sequoia Crest Wealth, LLC is valid and
   contains a single performance obligation for the subscription service. The
   fixed transaction price of $18,000 is recognized ratably over the 12-month
   term, while the variable Library Royalty fee is recognized as incurred.

   [MEDIUM] Variable consideration present
      Facts and circumstances:  usage-based pricing present; usage_model=base_plus_capped
      Question:                 Has the variable portion been identified and separated from any fixed fee? Is RevLens recognizing usage as it is invoiced/incurred (right-to-invoice), or does the arrangement require estimating variable consideration? Confirm the fixed portion's recognition is not inflated by unearned usage.
      Recommended procedure:    Confirm the variable portion is separated from the fixed fee and is recognised as usage occurs rather than estimated for the full term.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The contract includes usage-based pricing under the base_plus_capped
      model. It is important to confirm that the variable portion of the
      transaction price is separated from the fixed fee and recognized as usage
      occurs rather than being estimated for the full term, consistent with
      RevLens policy ASC 606-10-32-5; 55-18.

8. MATTERS CONSIDERED AND CONCLUDED IMMATERIAL

   No quantified matter was concluded immaterial on this contract.

9. COMPLETENESS OF REVIEW

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 1
   is reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 17 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Cedar Falls Investments, LLC represents a
   single performance obligation for subscription services with a fixed base
   fee and variable usage fees subject to a capped overage. The transaction
   price of $15,000 is recognized ratably over the 12-month term, while
   variable usage fees are recognized as incurred. There are no onboarding
   fees, discounts, or material rights affecting revenue recognition. The
   contract is valid and meets ASC 606 criteria for revenue recognition.

   Recognition status: Cleared for recognition; open items to be resolved before period close

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.
