Standard:            ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with Customers
Recognition status:  Not cleared for recognition pending resolution of the open items below
==============================================================================

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Lighthouse Point Advisors, LLC has entered into a 12-month subscription
   contract with a total fixed transaction price of $6,000, representing a
   monthly minimum fee of $500. The contract includes a usage-based fee of
   $0.03 per processed account transaction, with the customer obligated to
   pay the greater of the monthly minimum or actual usage each month. The
   contract is valid, contains no separately priced onboarding or
   implementation fees, and includes standard updates and support as part of
   the subscription. There is no discount or material right embedded in the
   contract, and the contract auto-renews at standard pricing.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-55-18              Right-to-invoice practical expedient
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract; Contract validity and collectibility
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-32-5; 55-18        Minimum commitment — floor fixed, excess variable

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Lighthouse Point
      Advisors, LLC, as it has defined parties, an effective date, subscription
      term, fees, payment terms, and termination provisions, with no
      collectibility concerns, consistent with ASC 606 and relevant guidance
      (ASC 606-10-25-1, ASC 606-10-25-1).

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, there is no separately priced onboarding.
      The contract contains a single performance obligation consisting of
      providing subscription access, including standard updates and support,
      which are not separately priced or distinct. There is no distinct
      onboarding or implementation service, and no additional distinct services
      are identified (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27).

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $6,000.00. The arrangement includes variable
      consideration (usage_with_floor) and no discount.
      The transaction price is $6,000, representing the fixed monthly minimum
      fee of $500 multiplied by the 12-month term. The usage-based fees are
      variable consideration and are excluded from the fixed transaction price,
      as they are recognized as incurred (ASC 606-10-32-5; 55-18). There is no
      discount or material right affecting the transaction price (ASC
      606-10-25-14(b); 25-27, ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      The entire fixed transaction price of $6,000 is allocated to the single
      performance obligation, as there are no other distinct goods or services
      to allocate to. The variable usage fees are accounted for separately and
      recognized as incurred (Variable consideration present, Minimum commitment
      floor).

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.
      Revenue related to the fixed monthly minimum fee is recognized ratably
      over the 12-month subscription term, reflecting transfer of control over
      time consistent with the subscription model (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27).
      Variable usage fees are recognized as usage occurs, constrained to the
      amount highly probable not to reverse, given the seasonal and
      unpredictable nature of usage (Unestimable or volatile usage). Billing
      occurs monthly in arrears, requiring assessment of contract assets or
      liabilities depending on timing of billing and performance (Billing timing
      balances).

5. REVENUE RECOGNITION SCHEDULE

   Mo  Period                            Billed       Revenue       Balance
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   1   05/01/2025 - 05/31/2025          $500.00       $500.00         $0.00

   3   07/01/2025 - 07/31/2025          $500.00       $500.00         $0.00

   Balance sheet account      Deferred revenue (contract liability), 2400 Deferred Revenue - Current

   Revenue is recognised on a straight-line monthly basis. Partial first or
   last months are not prorated by days; where the term starts mid-month this
   affects the timing pattern only, not the total transaction price.

6. JOURNAL ENTRIES

   On invoice
      Dr  1200 Accounts Receivable                            $500.00
          Cr  2400 Deferred Revenue - Current                 $500.00
      Record the contract liability on billing in advance of performance.

   Monthly, over the subscription term
      Dr  2400 Deferred Revenue - Current                     $500.00
          Cr  4100 Subscription Revenue                       $500.00
      Release the contract liability as the subscription is delivered.

   As usage is incurred
      Dr  1200 Accounts Receivable                        as incurred
          Cr  4300 Usage Revenue                          as incurred
      Usage-based consideration (usage_with_floor) is variable and is recognised as the usage occurs.

7. OPEN ITEMS AND MATTERS FOR FOLLOW-UP

   [HIGH] Unestimable or volatile usage
      Facts and circumstances:  usage with no fixed fee=False, volatile or seasonal=True, no basis for estimation=False
      Question:                 Has the variable consideration estimate been constrained to the amount highly probable not to reverse? Where usage is seasonal or unpredictable, is revenue recognized only as the usage occurs rather than estimated for the full term?
      Recommended procedure:    Constrain the variable consideration estimate to the amount highly probable not to reverse; recognise as usage occurs where volume is seasonal or unpredictable.
      Timing:                   Before revenue is recognised
      Disposition:              open
      Given the usage-based fees are variable and the usage volume is seasonal
      or unpredictable, the variable consideration estimate should be
      constrained to the amount highly probable not to reverse. Revenue for
      usage fees should be recognized as usage occurs.

   [MEDIUM] Billing timing balances
      Facts and circumstances:  the subscription itself is billed in arrears; billing_used=monthly_arrears
      Question:                 Where the customer is billed in advance, is a contract liability (deferred revenue) recorded and released as performance occurs? Where RevLens performs before it can bill (e.g., monthly in arrears), is a contract asset recorded rather than deferred revenue?
      Recommended procedure:    Confirm a contract liability is recorded where the customer is billed in advance, and a contract asset where performance precedes the right to bill.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      Since billing occurs monthly in arrears, it is important to confirm that a
      contract liability is recorded when the customer is billed in advance, and
      a contract asset is recorded when performance precedes the right to bill.

   [MEDIUM] Variable consideration present
      Facts and circumstances:  usage-based pricing present; usage_model=usage_with_floor
      Question:                 Has the variable portion been identified and separated from any fixed fee? Is RevLens recognizing usage as it is invoiced/incurred (right-to-invoice), or does the arrangement require estimating variable consideration? Confirm the fixed portion's recognition is not inflated by unearned usage.
      Recommended procedure:    Confirm the variable portion is separated from the fixed fee and is recognised as usage occurs rather than estimated for the full term.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The variable portion of the transaction price, representing usage-based
      fees, should be separated from the fixed fee and recognized as usage
      occurs rather than estimated for the full term.

   [MEDIUM] Minimum commitment floor
      Facts and circumstances:  a stated minimum or committed fee alongside usage above an included volume
      Question:                 Is the committed minimum being recognized as fixed consideration over the term, with only usage above the included volume treated as variable? Confirm the minimum is recognized regardless of actual usage.
      Recommended procedure:    Confirm the committed minimum is recognised as fixed consideration over the term, with only usage above the included volume treated as variable.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The committed minimum fee is recognized as fixed consideration over the
      term, with only usage above the minimum treated as variable consideration.

8. MATTERS CONSIDERED AND CONCLUDED IMMATERIAL

   No quantified matter was concluded immaterial on this contract.

9. COMPLETENESS OF REVIEW

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 4
   are reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 14 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   We have concluded that the subscription contract with Lighthouse Point
   Advisors, LLC contains one performance obligation for subscription access,
   with a fixed transaction price of $6,000 representing the committed
   monthly minimum fees over 12 months. Revenue for the fixed fees is
   recognized ratably over the subscription term, while variable usage fees
   are recognized as incurred, constrained to amounts not expected to
   reverse. The contract is valid, contains no material rights or discounts,
   and standard updates and support are included in the subscription. Billing
   occurs monthly in arrears, necessitating appropriate recognition of
   contract assets or liabilities based on timing differences between
   performance and billing.

   Recognition status: Not cleared for recognition pending resolution of the open items below

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Ironwood Asset Partners, LLC has entered into a contract for a
   subscription service with a base annual fee of $20,000. The contract term
   is 12 months, with payment due in advance within 30 days. The subscription
   includes a monthly allotment of 2,000 processed account transactions, with
   optional additional capacity blocks available for separate purchase at
   $750 per 1,000 transactions. The contract contains an automatic renewal
   clause at the then-current list pricing, with no stated renewal discount
   or price. Standard updates and support are included as part of the
   subscription but are not separately priced or distinct performance
   obligations. The contract is signed by both parties and is enforceable
   with defined rights and payment terms.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract; Contract validity and collectibility
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Ironwood Asset
      Partners, LLC, as it is signed by both parties, specifies the subscription
      term, fees, payment terms, and termination provisions, and meets the
      criteria for enforceability and probable collection under RevLens policy
      (ASC 606-10-25-1).

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, there is no separately priced onboarding.
      The contract contains a single performance obligation consisting of the
      base subscription service. There are no separately priced onboarding or
      implementation services, and standard updates and support are not distinct
      performance obligations per RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27).
      Optional capacity blocks are excluded from the base subscription and are
      not part of the performance obligation here.

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $20,000.00. The arrangement includes variable
      consideration (purchase_option) and no discount.
      The transaction price is $20,000, representing the base subscription fee
      for the 12-month term. There are no discounts or variable consideration
      components included in the transaction price. Optional capacity blocks and
      renewal pricing are excluded from the transaction price as they are either
      optional or contingent on future ordering.

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      Since there is only one performance obligation, the entire transaction
      price of $20,000 is allocated to the base subscription service. There is
      no need for allocation among multiple obligations.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.
      Revenue is recognised ratably over the 12-month subscription term,
      consistent with the transfer of control over time. The usage model is
      characterized as a purchase option because additional capacity blocks are
      optional and separately ordered, not automatically charged as overage.
      Therefore, revenue is recognised on a straight-line basis over the
      contract term in accordance with RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-14(b);
      25-27).

      Question:                 Has the variable portion been identified and separated from any fixed fee? Is RevLens recognizing usage as it is invoiced/incurred (right-to-invoice), or does the arrangement require estimating variable consideration? Confirm the fixed portion's recognition is not inflated by unearned usage.
      Recommended procedure:    Confirm the variable portion is separated from the fixed fee and is recognised as usage occurs rather than estimated for the full term.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      Given the presence of usage-based pricing under the base_plus_overage
      model, it is important to confirm that the variable consideration is
      appropriately separated from the fixed fee and recognized as usage occurs
      rather than being estimated for the full contract term.

8. MATTERS CONSIDERED AND CONCLUDED IMMATERIAL

   Proration partial period
      The item was triggered on the facts. The exposure is $140.00 against a
      materiality threshold for this contract of $1,000.00 (5% of the
      transaction price, floor $1,000.00). We consider the amount immaterial to
      the financial statements taken as a whole and no adjustment has been
      recorded.

9. COMPLETENESS OF REVIEW

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 1
   is reported at section 7, 1 at section 8, and 16 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Sprout Financial, LLC represents a valid
   subscription arrangement with a single performance obligation comprising
   both fixed and variable components. The fixed base subscription fee of
   $3,600 is recognized on a straight-line basis over the 12-month term,
   while variable usage fees are recognized as incurred. There are no
   discounts or material rights affecting the transaction price, and no
   separately priced onboarding or additional services. This accounting
   treatment aligns with ASC 606 and relevant RevLens policies.

   Recognition status: Cleared for recognition; open items to be resolved before period close

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Solstice Wealth Management, LLC has entered into a 12-month contract for a
   hosted subscription service with a fixed base subscription fee of $48,000
   per year and additional usage fees charged at $0.20 per transaction above
   a monthly base allotment. The contract includes standard updates and
   support but no separately priced onboarding or implementation services.
   There are no discounts or material rights embedded in the contract, and
   the contract is valid with defined payment terms and no collectibility
   concerns.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-55-18              Right-to-invoice practical expedient
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract; Contract validity and collectibility
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-32-5; 55-18        Usage/overage — as incurred (right-to-invoice); Minimum commitment — floor fixed, excess variable

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Solstice Wealth
      Management, LLC as it is signed by both parties, includes defined fees and
      payment terms, and meets the criteria for contract validity under ASC 606
      and RevLens policy. There are no indications of collectibility issues, and
      the contract grants enforceable rights and obligations.

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, there is no separately priced onboarding.
      The contract contains a single performance obligation consisting of the
      hosted subscription service with standard updates and support. There are
      no distinct onboarding or implementation services, nor any additional
      distinct services, as these are not separately priced or separately
      purchasable under the contract terms.

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $48,000.00. The arrangement includes variable
      consideration (base_plus_overage) and no discount.
      The transaction price is $48,000, representing the fixed base subscription
      fee for the 12-month term. Usage fees are variable consideration charged
      at $0.20 per transaction above the monthly base allotment and are excluded
      from the fixed transaction price as they are recognized as incurred. There
      are no discounts or material rights affecting the transaction price.

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      The entire fixed transaction price of $48,000 is allocated to the single
      performance obligation, as there are no other distinct goods or services
      to allocate to. The variable usage fees are accounted for separately and
      recognized as incurred.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.
      Revenue from the fixed base subscription fee is recognized ratably over
      the 12-month contract term, reflecting the continuous transfer of the
      hosted service over time. Variable usage fees are recognized as incurred
      based on actual usage, consistent with the base plus overage pricing model
      and guidance on variable consideration.

5. REVENUE RECOGNITION SCHEDULE

   ------------------------------------------------------------------------------

   Revenue is recognised on a straight-line monthly basis. Partial first or

   affects the timing pattern only, not the total transaction price.

6. JOURNAL ENTRIES

   On invoice
      Dr  1200 Accounts Receivable                         $48,000.00
          Cr  2400 Deferred Revenue - Current              $48,000.00
      Record the contract liability on billing in advance of performance.

   Monthly, over the subscription term
      Dr  2400 Deferred Revenue - Current                   $4,000.00
          Cr  4100 Subscription Revenue                     $4,000.00
      Release the contract liability as the subscription is delivered.

   As usage is incurred
      Dr  1200 Accounts Receivable                        as incurred
          Cr  4300 Usage Revenue                          as incurred
      Usage-based consideration (base_plus_overage) is variable and is recognised as the usage occurs.

7. OPEN ITEMS AND MATTERS FOR FOLLOW-UP

   [HIGH] Unestimable or volatile usage
      Facts and circumstances:  usage with no fixed fee=False, volatile or seasonal=True, no basis for estimation=False
      Question:                 Has the variable consideration estimate been constrained to the amount highly probable not to reverse? Where usage is seasonal or unpredictable, is revenue recognized only as the usage occurs rather than estimated for the full term?
      Recommended procedure:    Constrain the variable consideration estimate to the amount highly probable not to reverse; recognise as usage occurs where volume is seasonal or unpredictable.
      Timing:                   Before revenue is recognised
      Disposition:              open
      Given the usage fees are variable and the volume is seasonal or
      unpredictable, it is necessary to constrain the variable consideration
      estimate to the amount that is highly probable not to reverse. Revenue for
      usage fees should be recognized as usage occurs to reflect the actual
      transfer of control and avoid overstatement.

   [MEDIUM] Variable consideration present
      Facts and circumstances:  usage-based pricing present; usage_model=base_plus_overage
      Question:                 Has the variable portion been identified and separated from any fixed fee? Is RevLens recognizing usage as it is invoiced/incurred (right-to-invoice), or does the arrangement require estimating variable consideration? Confirm the fixed portion's recognition is not inflated by unearned usage.
      Recommended procedure:    Confirm the variable portion is separated from the fixed fee and is recognised as usage occurs rather than estimated for the full term.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The presence of usage-based pricing under a base plus overage model
      requires confirmation that the variable portion is separated from the
      fixed fee and recognized as usage occurs rather than being estimated for
      the full contract term. This ensures compliance with ASC 606 guidance on
      variable consideration.

8. MATTERS CONSIDERED AND CONCLUDED IMMATERIAL

   No quantified matter was concluded immaterial on this contract.

9. COMPLETENESS OF REVIEW

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 2
   are reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 16 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Solstice Wealth Management, LLC is valid and
   contains one performance obligation for the hosted subscription service.
   The transaction price consists of a fixed base subscription fee of
   $48,000, which is recognized ratably over the 12-month term. Variable
   usage fees are excluded from the fixed transaction price and recognized as
   incurred. There are no discounts or material rights embedded in the
   contract. This accounting treatment aligns with ASC 606 and relevant
   RevLens policies. The variable consideration related to usage fees is
   subject to review to ensure appropriate recognition consistent with the
   guidance on volatile or seasonal usage patterns.

   Recognition status: Not cleared for recognition pending resolution of the open items below

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

   $2,000, with a $3,000 discount applied solely to the premium support
   component. The contract is valid, signed by both parties, and includes
   payment terms payable in advance within 30 days, termination provisions,
   and auto-renewal at then-current list prices.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview

   be read together with them.

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Brookstone Wealth
      Advisors, LLC as it is signed by both parties, specifies defined fees and
      payment terms, includes termination provisions, and has probable
      collectibility. The contract term is 12 months with auto-renewal at then-
      current list prices, which does not create a material right.

   Identify the performance obligations
      2 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription,
      premium_support(distinct). On onboarding, there is no separately priced
      onboarding.
      The contract contains two distinct performance obligations: the platform
      subscription and the premium support service. The premium support is
      separately priced and distinct from the standard support and updates
      included in the subscription. There is no separately priced onboarding or
      implementation service, so no onboarding performance obligation exists.

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $22,000.00. The arrangement includes no variable

      subscription fee and a $2,000 premium support fee. A $3,000 discount is
      applied solely to the premium support component, which is supported by
      observable evidence in the contract. There is no variable consideration or
      usage-based pricing; fees are fixed and recurring.

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations

      The transaction price is allocated to the two performance obligations
      based on their standalone selling prices. Given the observable evidence
      that the $3,000 discount applies exclusively to the premium support
      service, the discount is treated as a fixed directed discount and
      allocated solely to that performance obligation in accordance with RevLens
      policy.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.
      Revenue is recognised over time on a ratable basis over the 12-month
      contract term for both the platform subscription and premium support

      2 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription,
      material_right(renewal). On onboarding, there is no separately priced
      onboarding.
      The contract contains two performance obligations. The first is the annual
      subscription service, which includes standard updates and support that are
      not distinct (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27). The second is a material right
      arising from the renewal option priced at $12,000, which is significantly
      below the standard list price of $24,000 for comparable customers,
      constituting a separate performance obligation under ASC 606 guidance (ASC
      606-10-25-27, ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45).

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $24,000.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and a discount characterised as renewal_option.
      The transaction price is $24,000 for the initial 12-month term,
      representing a fixed annual subscription fee with no usage-based or
      variable components (ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45). There are no additional
      fee components or stated totals; therefore, the transaction price is
      $24,000.

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      Allocation across performance obligations by relative standalone selling
      price (SSP) is NOT implemented in this analysis; a multi-obligation
      schedule must be reallocated before use.
      The transaction price is allocated between the two performance obligations
      based on their standalone selling prices. The initial subscription fee is
      allocated to the first performance obligation, and the discounted renewal
      option, representing a material right, is allocated separately in
      accordance with RevLens policy and ASC 606 guidance (ASC 606-10-55-42 to
      55-45, ASC 606-10-25-27).

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.
      Revenue for the subscription service is recognised ratably over the
      12-month contract term, reflecting transfer of control over time. The
      material right related to the renewal option is accounted for separately,
      with recognition considerations to be reviewed prior to recognition,
      consistent with the contract terms and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-55-42 to
      55-45).

For an upfront fee waived on renewal, has the material-right question been assessed?

      Timing:                   Before revenue is recognised
      Disposition:              open
      Given the identification of a material right related to the discounted
      renewal option, it is necessary to obtain the renewal prices offered to
      comparable customers to confirm whether the option conveys a material
      right requiring separate allocation, as per Material right renewal or fee.

   We have concluded that the contract with Marigold Capital Group, LLC is
   valid and contains two performance obligations: the annual subscription
   service and a material right arising from a discounted renewal option. The
   transaction price of $24,000 is fixed and allocated between these
   obligations based on their standalone selling prices. Revenue for the
   subscription service will be recognised ratably over the 12-month term,
   while the material right will be accounted for separately. This accounting
   treatment aligns with ASC 606 guidance and RevLens policies.

   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Halcyon Financial Partners, LLC has entered into a contract for a 12-month
   subscription to a platform service with a total transaction price of
   $17,000. This amount comprises a $15,000 recurring annual platform
   subscription fee and a one-time $2,000 account activation fee. The
   contract includes standard updates and support as part of the subscription
   and contains no usage-based pricing or discounts. Renewal occurs
   automatically at the then-current list price without any stated discounted
   renewal price.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-55-50 to 55-51     Nonrefundable upfront fees

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists under ASC 606 as the
      parties are committed, the rights and payment terms are identifiable, and
      collection is probable. The contract specifies defined fees, payment
      terms, termination provisions for breach, and automatic renewal,
      satisfying the criteria for contract validity (ASC 606-10-05-4).

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, there is no separately priced onboarding.
      The contract contains a single performance obligation, which is the
      provision of the platform subscription service over the 12-month term.
      There is no separately priced onboarding or implementation fee to assess
      as a distinct performance obligation, and standard updates and support are
      integrated within the subscription service and are not distinct (ASC
      606-10-25-14(b); 25-27).

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $17,000.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and no discount.
      The transaction price is fixed at $17,000, consisting of a $15,000 annual
      subscription fee and a $2,000 one-time account activation fee. There are
      no variable consideration components or discounts, and the total
      transaction price equals the sum of the fees (ASC 606-10-55-50 to 55-51).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      Since there is only one performance obligation, the entire transaction
      price of $17,000 is allocated to that obligation. There is no material
      right related to renewal pricing because the renewal occurs at the then-
      current list price without any discount, and thus no separate allocation
      is required (ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45, ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45).

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.
      Revenue is recognised over time on a ratable basis over the 12-month
      contract term, consistent with the nature of the subscription service and
      the transfer of control over time.

   [HIGH] Material right renewal or fee
      Facts and circumstances:  nonrefundable upfront fee=True; discounted renewal option=False; material_right=FALSE
      Question:                 Does the renewal/option price sit significantly below the price offered to comparable customers (a material right), requiring a portion of the transaction price to be allocated to it and recognized at exercise or expiry? For an upfront fee waived on renewal, has the material-right question been assessed?

      renewal pricing is at the then-current list price, it is recommended to
      obtain the renewal price offered to comparable customers to confirm
      whether the renewal option conveys a material right requiring separate
      allocation.

   [MEDIUM] Bundled onboarding distinctness
      Facts and circumstances:  separately priced onboarding fee of 2000.0 alongside subscription of 15000.0
      Question:                 Is the onboarding customized to the customer and required before the customer can access the platform (suggesting it is not distinct and should be combined), or is it standardized, optional, and available elsewhere (suggesting a separate performance obligation)? Confirm whether the onboarding fee is being front-loaded rather than recognized over the service period.
      Recommended procedure:    Document whether the onboarding is customised and required for platform access (combine into the subscription obligation) or standardised and optional (separate obligation). Retain the evidence relied on.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The contract includes a separately priced onboarding fee of $2,000
      alongside the $15,000 subscription fee. It is advisable to document
      whether the onboarding service is customised and required for platform
      access, which would support combining it into the subscription obligation,
      or if it is standardised and optional, which would support treating it as
      a separate performance obligation.

   single performance obligation for a 12-month platform subscription service

   subscription service. There is no material right associated with renewal

   RevLens policies.

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Atlas Summit Advisors, LLC entered into a contract with a total
   transaction price of $100,000 for a bundle of services including
   subscription with implementation, premium support, and advisory
   consulting. The contract term is 12 months, and the contract is valid and
   enforceable with specified payment terms and auto-renewal provisions. The
   contract includes a fixed prorata discount applied to the bundle, reducing
   the combined standalone selling prices from $125,000 to $100,000. The
   implementation services are customized and required for access, and
   premium support and advisory consulting are separately priced and distinct
   from the subscription.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract
   ASC 606-10-25-21              Separately identifiable (factors)
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-21; 55-51  Onboarding — non-distinct by default
   ASC 606-10-32-36 to 32-37     Discount allocation — pro-rata default vs directed

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Atlas Summit Advisors,
      LLC, as the contract specifies identifiable rights, payment terms, and
      there is no indication of collectibility issues or at-will cancellation
      without compensation, consistent with ASC 606 guidance (ASC 606-10-25-1).

   Identify the performance obligations
      3 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription,
      premium_support(distinct), advisory_consulting(distinct). On onboarding,
      onboarding is not distinct and is combined into the subscription.
      The contract contains three performance obligations: the subscription
      combined with implementation services, premium support, and advisory
      consulting. Implementation services are not distinct from the subscription
      because they are customized and required for access, per ASC 606 (ASC
      606-10-25-21) and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-21; 55-51).
      Premium support and advisory consulting are distinct services as they are
      separately priced and separately purchasable, consistent with RevLens
      policy (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27).

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $100,000.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and a discount characterised as fixed_prorata.
      The transaction price is $100,000, reflecting a fixed prorata discount of
      $25,000 applied to the bundle, reducing the combined standalone selling
      prices from $125,000 to $100,000. The contract does not include any usage-
      based or variable consideration, and the discount is not attributed to any
      specific component, consistent with RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-32-36 to
      32-37).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      Allocation across performance obligations by relative standalone selling
      price (SSP) is NOT implemented in this analysis; a multi-obligation
      schedule must be reallocated before use.
      The transaction price should be allocated to the three performance
      obligations based on their relative standalone selling prices, applying
      the fixed prorata discount proportionately, as the discount applies to the
      bundle as a whole without attribution, in accordance with ASC 606 guidance
      (Discount allocation method).

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised the subscription ratably over time and the
      onboarding at a point in time over 12 months.
      Revenue for the subscription with implementation services is recognised
      over time on a ratable basis over the 12-month term, while revenue for the
      onboarding (implementation) component is recognised at a point in time.
      Premium support and advisory consulting are recognised according to their
      respective patterns, consistent with the recognition methods identified.
      There is no material right related to renewal options, as the contract
      auto-renews at then-current list pricing without discount, per ASC 606
      (ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45) and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-55-42 to
      55-45).

7. OPEN ITEMS AND MATTERS FOR FOLLOW-UP

   [MEDIUM] Discount allocation method
      Facts and circumstances:  combined standalone price 125000.0 exceeds the bundled total 100000.0; discount_type=fixed_prorata
      Question:                 Is the discount allocated proportionately across all performance obligations by default? A discount is directed to a specific obligation only where RevLens regularly sells each item separately and regularly sells the discounted item on its own at a comparable discount — is that evidence present?
      Recommended procedure:    Obtain the standalone selling price of each component and allocate the discount proportionately unless the directed-discount evidence is present.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The combined standalone selling price of $125,000 exceeds the bundled
      total of $100,000, indicating a discount. The discount is classified as
      fixed prorata because it applies to the bundle as a whole without
      attribution to any component. It is recommended to obtain the standalone
      selling price of each component and allocate the discount proportionately
      unless directed-discount evidence is present.

   [MEDIUM] Multiple performance obligations
      Facts and circumstances:  num_POs=3; recognition_method=sub:over_time_ratable; onboarding:point_in_time
      Question:                 Has the transaction price been allocated across all obligations by relative SSP, and is each obligation recognized on its correct pattern (over time for the subscription; at completion for distinct one-time deliverables; by effort/hours for professional services)?
      Recommended procedure:    Allocate the transaction price across all obligations by relative standalone selling price and confirm each is recognised on its own pattern.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      There are three performance obligations with differing recognition
      methods: subscription recognised over time on a ratable basis, and
      onboarding recognised at a point in time. It is recommended to allocate
      the transaction price across all obligations by relative standalone
      selling price and confirm each is recognised on its own pattern.

   are reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 16 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   We have concluded that the contract with Atlas Summit Advisors, LLC
   includes three performance obligations: a subscription combined with
   implementation services, premium support, and advisory consulting. The
   transaction price of $100,000 reflects a fixed prorata discount applied to
   the bundle. The transaction price should be allocated to each performance
   obligation based on their relative standalone selling prices. Revenue for
   the subscription with implementation is recognised over time on a ratable
   basis, with onboarding recognised at a point in time, and the other
   services recognised according to their respective patterns. No material
   right exists related to renewal options. The contract is valid and
   enforceable, and the accounting treatment aligns with ASC 606 and relevant
   RevLens policies.

      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Redwood Crossing
      Capital, LLC, as it is signed by both parties, specifies payment terms,
      and has no collectibility concerns, consistent with ASC 606 and RevLens
      policy (ASC 606-10-25-1).

   Identify the performance obligations
      2 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription,
      premium_support(distinct). On onboarding, there is no separately priced
      onboarding.
      The contract contains two distinct performance obligations: the
      subscription service and the separately priced premium support. The
      subscription does not include a separately priced onboarding or
      implementation service, and standard support is excluded as a distinct
      service. Premium support is distinct and regularly sold separately,
      meeting the criteria for separate performance obligations (ASC
      606-10-25-14(b); 25-27).

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $60,000.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and a discount characterised as fixed_prorata.
      The transaction price is $60,000 for the full 24-month term, representing
      a fixed fee that includes both subscription and premium support
      components. This amount is below the combined standalone selling prices of
      $66,000, reflecting a volume discount applied to the package as a whole
      (ASC 606-10-32-36 to 32-37, ASC 606-10-32-36 to 32-37).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      Allocation across performance obligations by relative standalone selling
      price (SSP) is NOT implemented in this analysis; a multi-obligation
      schedule must be reallocated before use.
      The discount is allocated on a fixed prorated basis across the bundled
      components, as the contract does not provide observable evidence to
      allocate the discount differently. This approach aligns with ASC 606 and
      RevLens policy requiring proportional allocation of discounts unless
      directed otherwise (ASC 606-10-32-36 to 32-37, ASC 606-10-32-36 to 32-37).

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 24 months.
      Revenue is recognised over time on a ratable basis over the 24-month term,
      consistent with the nature of the subscription and premium support
      services, which transfer control continuously to the customer throughout
      the contract period.

A discount is directed to a specific obligation only where RevLens regularly sells each item separately and regularly sells the discounted item on its own at a comparable discount — is that evidence present?

      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The combined standalone selling price of $66,000 exceeds the bundled total
      of $60,000, resulting in a volume discount. The discount allocation method
      is fixed prorated across the components. It is recommended to obtain the
      standalone selling price of each component and allocate the discount

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Vantage Hill Investments, LLC has entered into a 12-month contract with a
   total transaction price of $40,000. The contract includes two performance
   obligations: a Platform Subscription and Premium Support. The Premium
   Support service is separately priced at $8,000 annually and is distinct
   from the Platform Subscription, which has no observable standalone selling
   price. The contract is valid, signed by both parties, with defined payment
   terms and termination provisions. There are no discounts, usage-based
   fees, or material rights embedded in the contract. Revenue is to be
   recognized ratably over the 12-month term.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract; Contract validity and collectibility
   ASC 606-10-25-27              Recognition over time
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Vantage Hill
      Investments, LLC, as it is signed by both parties, includes defined
      payment terms payable in advance within 30 days, and contains termination
      provisions for breach with notice. These criteria satisfy the requirements
      of ASC 606 and RevLens policy for contract identification (ASC
      606-10-25-1, ASC 606-10-25-1).

   Identify the performance obligations
      2 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription,
      premium_support(distinct). On onboarding, there is no separately priced
      onboarding.
      The contract contains two distinct performance obligations: the Platform
      Subscription and Premium Support. Premium Support is separately priced at
      an observable standalone selling price of $8,000 annually and is distinct
      from the Platform Subscription, which lacks an observable standalone
      selling price. There is no separately priced onboarding or implementation
      service, and no material rights exist as renewal terms specify auto-
      renewal at then-current list pricing without discounts (ASC
      606-10-25-14(b); 25-27, ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45).

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $40,000.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and no discount.
      The transaction price is $40,000 for the 12-month term, comprising $8,000
      for Premium Support and $32,000 for the Platform Subscription. This total
      reflects the bundled amount stated in the contract with no discounts or
      reductions from list prices. The Platform Subscription price is treated as
      residual due to the absence of an observable standalone selling price,
      consistent with ASC 606 and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      Allocation across performance obligations by relative standalone selling
      price (SSP) is NOT implemented in this analysis; a multi-obligation
      schedule must be reallocated before use.
      The transaction price is allocated between the two performance obligations
      by applying the residual approach to the Platform Subscription component,
      which lacks an observable standalone selling price, and using the
      observable standalone selling price for Premium Support. This allocation
      aligns with ASC 606 guidance and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-14(b);
      25-27).

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.
      Revenue is recognized ratably over the 12-month contract term, reflecting
      the transfer of control over time for both performance obligations. This
      recognition method is appropriate given the nature of the subscription and
      support services and consistent with ASC 606 (ASC 606-10-05-4).

   Coral Gables Wealth, LLC has entered into a contract for a subscription
   service with a total transaction price of $12,000. The contract term is 13
   months, during which the customer receives access to the service. The fee
   structure reflects a promotional offer providing 13 months of access for
   the price of 12 months, effectively granting one month free. The contract
   includes standard updates and support as part of the subscription, with no
   separately priced onboarding or implementation fees. The contract is
   valid, signed by both parties, with defined payment terms and termination
   provisions.

   ASC 606-10-32-28 to 32-33     Allocation based on standalone selling price

   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Material rights — discounted renewals/options

   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Coral Gables Wealth,
      LLC, as it is signed by both parties, includes defined payment terms
      requiring payment within 30 days of the effective date, and contains
      termination provisions for breach with notice. The contract meets the
      criteria for enforceability and probable collectibility as outlined in
      RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-1).

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, there is no separately priced onboarding.
      The contract contains a single performance obligation consisting of the
      subscription service access over the 13-month term. There are no
      separately priced onboarding or implementation services, and standard
      updates and support are not distinct performance obligations per RevLens
      policy (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27). There is no material right arising
      from renewal terms, as renewal pricing is at then-current list prices,
      consistent with ASC 606 (ASC 606-10-32-28 to 32-33) and RevLens policy
      (ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45).

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $12,000.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and a discount characterised as free_period.
      The transaction price is $12,000, representing a single recurring annual
      fee that covers the entire 13-month access period, including the free
      month granted as a promotional offer. This fee structure is consistent
      with the contract terms and fee components, and future renewal fees are
      excluded from the transaction price as per the fee components analysis.

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      Since there is only one performance obligation, the entire transaction
      price of $12,000 is allocated to that single obligation. There are no
      additional distinct services or fee components requiring allocation.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 13 months.
      Revenue is recognized ratably over the 13-month contract term, reflecting
      the transfer of control of the subscription service over time. The free
      additional month is treated as a free_period discount type, and revenue

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Thornfield Asset Management, LLC entered into a 12-month subscription
   contract with RevLens for platform access and onboarding services. The
   total transaction price is $20,500, comprising an $18,000 recurring annual
   subscription fee and a $2,500 one-time onboarding fee. The contract
   includes a renewal option at a discounted price of $12,000, which is
   significantly below the standard renewal price of $18,000 and is exclusive
   to this customer. The contract is valid with defined parties, terms, and
   payment conditions, and there are no collectibility concerns. Standard
   updates and support are included in the subscription but are not distinct
   performance obligations.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-22     Onboarding — distinct (exception)

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Thornfield Asset
      Management, LLC, as it meets the criteria of defined parties, effective
      date, subscription term, fees, payment terms, termination provisions, and
      collectibility, consistent with ASC 606 and RevLens policy (Bundled
      onboarding distinctness, ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-22).

   Identify the performance obligations
      3 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription,
      onboarding(distinct), material_right(renewal). On onboarding, the
      onboarding is a distinct performance obligation.
      The contract contains three performance obligations. The onboarding
      service is distinct and accounted for separately because platform access
      does not depend on RevLens performing the onboarding implementation,
      satisfying the distinct-service exception (Bundled onboarding
      distinctness). The subscription service, including standard updates and
      support, is a single performance obligation. The renewal option conveys a
      material right due to the significantly discounted renewal price exclusive
      to this customer, requiring separate consideration (ASC 606-10-55-42 to
      55-45, Material right renewal or fee).

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $20,500.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and a discount characterised as renewal_option.
      The transaction price is $20,500, consisting of the $18,000 subscription
      fee and the $2,500 onboarding fee. The discounted renewal price of $12,000
      is excluded from the transaction price as it pertains to a future term and
      represents a material right rather than a discount on the initial
      transaction price (Discount allocation method, ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45).
      There is no usage-based pricing; fees are fixed and determinable (Bundled
      onboarding distinctness).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      Allocation across performance obligations by relative standalone selling
      price (SSP) is NOT implemented in this analysis; a multi-obligation
      schedule must be reallocated before use.
      The transaction price is allocated to the three performance obligations
      based on their relative standalone selling prices. The onboarding fee is
      separately priced at $2,500, and the subscription fee is $18,000. The
      renewal option's material right requires allocation of a portion of the
      transaction price to reflect the value of the discounted renewal,
      consistent with ASC 606 guidance (Multiple performance obligations,
      Discount allocation method).

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised the subscription ratably over time and the
      onboarding at a point in time over 12 months.
      Revenue for the subscription performance obligation is recognised ratably
      over the 12-month term, reflecting the continuous transfer of control over
      time. The onboarding performance obligation is recognised at a point in
      time upon completion of the onboarding service. The material right related
      to the renewal option is accounted for as a separate performance
      obligation and recognised when the renewal occurs or as the right is
      exercised (Multiple performance obligations).

   ------------------------------------------------------------------------------

   On invoice

          Cr  4100 Subscription Revenue                     $1,500.00

7. OPEN ITEMS AND MATTERS FOR FOLLOW-UP

   [HIGH] Material right renewal or fee
      Facts and circumstances:  nonrefundable upfront fee=False; discounted renewal option=True; material_right=TRUE
      Question:                 Does the renewal/option price sit significantly below the price offered to comparable customers (a material right), requiring a portion of the transaction price to be allocated to it and recognized at exercise or expiry? For an upfront fee waived on renewal, has the material-right question been assessed?
      Recommended procedure:    Obtain the renewal price offered to comparable customers and assess whether the option conveys a material right requiring separate allocation.
      Timing:                   Before revenue is recognised
      Disposition:              open
      The renewal option conveys a material right due to the significantly
      discounted renewal price exclusive to this customer. It is necessary to
      obtain the renewal price offered to comparable customers to confirm
      whether the option requires separate allocation as a material right.

   [MEDIUM] Bundled onboarding distinctness
      Facts and circumstances:  separately priced onboarding fee of 2500.0 alongside subscription of 18000.0
      Question:                 Is the onboarding customized to the customer and required before the customer can access the platform (suggesting it is not distinct and should be combined), or is it standardized, optional, and available elsewhere (suggesting a separate performance obligation)? Confirm whether the onboarding fee is being front-loaded rather than recognized over the service period.
      Recommended procedure:    Document whether the onboarding is customised and required for platform access (combine into the subscription obligation) or standardised and optional (separate obligation). Retain the evidence relied on.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The onboarding fee is separately priced at $2,500 alongside the $18,000
      subscription fee. Documentation should confirm whether onboarding is
      standardised and optional, supporting its classification as a separate
      performance obligation.

   [MEDIUM] Discount allocation method
      Facts and circumstances:  combined standalone price 20500.0 exceeds the bundled total 20500.0; discount_type=renewal_option
      Question:                 Is the discount allocated proportionately across all performance obligations by default? A discount is directed to a specific obligation only where RevLens regularly sells each item separately and regularly sells the discounted item on its own at a comparable discount — is that evidence present?
      Recommended procedure:    Obtain the standalone selling price of each component and allocate the discount proportionately unless the directed-discount evidence is present.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The combined standalone price equals the bundled total of $20,500, with
      the discount type identified as a renewal option. It is important to
      obtain standalone selling prices for each component and allocate the
      transaction price proportionately unless evidence supports a different
      discount allocation.

   [MEDIUM] Multiple performance obligations
      Facts and circumstances:  num_POs=3; recognition_method=sub:over_time_ratable; onboarding:point_in_time
      Question:                 Has the transaction price been allocated across all obligations by relative SSP, and is each obligation recognized on its correct pattern (over time for the subscription; at completion for distinct one-time deliverables; by effort/hours for professional services)?
      Recommended procedure:    Allocate the transaction price across all obligations by relative standalone selling price and confirm each is recognised on its own pattern.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      With three performance obligations identified, the transaction price
      should be allocated by relative standalone selling price, and revenue
      recognition should follow the distinct pattern for each obligation,
      including ratable recognition for subscription and point-in-time
      recognition for onboarding.

8. MATTERS CONSIDERED AND CONCLUDED IMMATERIAL

   No quantified matter was concluded immaterial on this contract.

9. COMPLETENESS OF REVIEW

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 4
   are reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 14 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Thornfield Asset Management, LLC is valid
   and contains three performance obligations: onboarding, subscription, and
   a material right related to a discounted renewal option. The transaction
   price of $20,500 is allocated based on standalone selling prices,
   excluding the renewal option price which is accounted for separately.
   Revenue is recognised over time for the subscription and at a point in
   time for onboarding, with the material right accounted for in accordance
   with ASC 606. This treatment aligns with RevLens policy and relevant
   accounting guidance.

   Recognition status: Not cleared for recognition pending resolution of the open items below

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Silvercrest Advisory, LLC entered into a contract with a total transaction
   price of $40,000 for a 12-month term. The contract includes two
   performance obligations: a platform subscription and advisor training. The
   platform subscription fee is $36,000 after a fixed directed discount of
   $4,000 from the list price of $40,000. Advisor training is priced
   separately at $4,000 and is available for separate purchase. The contract
   is valid, signed by both parties, with payment terms requiring payment
   within 30 days. There is no usage-based pricing or onboarding services
   included in the contract. The contract also contains an auto-renewal
   clause at then-current list pricing, which does not confer a material
   right to the customer.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-32-36 to 32-37     Allocating a discount; Discount allocation — pro-rata default vs directed
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Contract validity and collectibility

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Silvercrest Advisory,
      LLC, as it is signed by both parties, includes identifiable rights and
      payment terms, and there are no indications of collectibility issues or
      cancellation without consequence, consistent with RevLens policy (ASC
      606-10-25-1).

   Identify the performance obligations
      2 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription, advisor
      training(distinct). On onboarding, there is no separately priced
      onboarding.
      The contract contains two performance obligations: the platform
      subscription and advisor training. The platform subscription is treated as
      a single performance obligation that includes updates and support, with no
      distinct onboarding or implementation services, as supported by the
      contract facts and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27). Advisor
      training is a distinct service priced separately and available for
      separate purchase, thus constituting a separate performance obligation.

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $40,000.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and a discount characterised as fixed_directed.
      The transaction price is $40,000, comprising $36,000 for the platform
      subscription after a fixed directed discount of $4,000 from the list price
      of $40,000, and $4,000 for advisor training. Since the contract term is 12
      months, the stated total of $40,000 is the transaction price, consistent
      with the contract facts and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-32-36 to 32-37).
      There is no variable consideration or usage-based pricing involved.

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      Allocation across performance obligations by relative standalone selling
      price (SSP) is NOT implemented in this analysis; a multi-obligation
      schedule must be reallocated before use.
      The transaction price is allocated to the two performance obligations. The
      discount of $4,000 is fixed and directed solely to the platform
      subscription, as explicitly stated in the contract and supported by ASC
      606 guidance (ASC 606-10-32-36 to 32-37) and RevLens policy (ASC
      606-10-32-36 to 32-37). Therefore, the discount is allocated entirely to
      the platform subscription, and advisor training is allocated its
      standalone selling price of $4,000.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised the subscription ratably over time and the
      onboarding at a point in time over 12 months.
      Revenue for the platform subscription is recognized over time on a ratable
      basis over the 12-month contract term, reflecting the continuous transfer
      of control. Revenue for advisor training is recognized at a point in time
      upon delivery of the training service. There is no material right related
      to renewal pricing, as the auto-renewal clause provides renewal at then-
      current list pricing, which does not confer a material right under ASC 606
      (ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45) and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-55-42 to
      55-45).

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the

   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Granite Peak Advisors, LLC has entered into a contract involving a single
   fixed annual subscription fee of $18,000. The contract is signed by both
   parties, includes identifiable rights and payment terms, and collection is
   probable. The contract provides for automatic renewal at then-current list
   pricing without any discounts or material rights. Standard updates and
   support are included as part of the subscription but are not separately
   priced or distinct performance obligations.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract; Contract validity and collectibility
   ASC 606-10-25-27              Recognition over time
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Granite Peak Advisors,
      LLC as it is signed by both parties, includes identifiable rights and
      payment terms, and collection is probable. There are no terms indicating
      cancellation at will or doubtful collectibility, consistent with ASC 606
      (ASC 606-10-25-1) and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-1).

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, there is no separately priced onboarding.
      The contract contains a single performance obligation consisting of the
      subscription service. There are no separately priced onboarding or
      implementation fees, and standard updates and support are included as part
      of the subscription and are not distinct performance obligations, in
      accordance with RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27).

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $18,000.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and no discount.
      The transaction price is $18,000, representing a single fixed annual
      subscription fee payable in advance. There are no variable consideration
      components, discounts, or other fee components. The contract does not
      include any material rights as renewal pricing is at then-current list
      pricing, consistent with ASC 606 (ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45) and RevLens
      policy (ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      Since there is only one performance obligation, the entire transaction
      price of $18,000 is allocated to that single performance obligation.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised on a basis to be determined over 0 months.
      The recognition method is currently undetermined because the subscription
      term length is not specified in the contract or facts provided. Although
      the fee is annual and prepaid, the absence of an explicitly stated service
      period prevents establishing the timing of revenue recognition. ASC 606
      (ASC 606-10-25-27) and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27)
      indicate subscription fees are recognized ratably over the service period,
      but the service period must be identified to apply this guidance.

   No recognition schedule produced: the service period could not be
   determined from the contract (see the open item on the missing service
   period or dates).

   None produced; see the schedule note above.

      Question:                 Can the recognition period and start date be determined from the contract? If the term or go-live is unspecified, a recognition schedule should not be produced; the missing term should be flagged and obtained.
      Recommended procedure:    Obtain the subscription term and go-live date from the customer. Do not issue a recognition schedule until the service period is established.

   We have concluded that the contract with Granite Peak Advisors, LLC is
