==============================================================================

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Driftwood Capital Partners, LLC entered into a contract for a 12-month
   subscription to access a platform, with a stated annual fee of $24,000.
   The contract does not include any separately priced onboarding or
   implementation fees, usage-based charges, discounts, or additional
   distinct services beyond standard updates and support. Renewal terms and
   pricing are not specified, and payment and cancellation terms remain to be
   agreed upon. These facts form the basis for the revenue recognition
   analysis under ASC 606.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract; Contract validity and collectibility
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Material rights — discounted renewals/options

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      Contract validity has not been established (status: FALSE).
      The contract does not meet the criteria for a valid contract under ASC 606
      because essential elements such as identifiable payment terms and
      cancellation provisions are absent. The parties have not established
      invoicing arrangements or a payment schedule, and either party may
      discontinue the contract at will without defined consequences.
      Consequently, the contract lacks the necessary commitment and commercial
      substance required for revenue recognition (ASC 606-10-25-1, ASC
      606-10-25-1).

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, there is no separately priced onboarding.
      There is a single performance obligation consisting of providing access to
      the platform subscription, which includes standard updates and support. No
      separately priced onboarding or implementation services exist, and no
      additional distinct services are identified (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27).

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $0.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and no discount.
      Although the stated annual subscription fee is $24,000 for the 12-month
      term, the transaction price for revenue recognition purposes is zero
      because the contract is not valid. The absence of agreed payment terms
      precludes recognition of the stated fee (ASC 606-10-25-1).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      Since there is only one performance obligation and the transaction price
      is zero due to contract invalidity, no allocation of the transaction price
      is necessary.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised on a basis to be determined over 12 months.
      Revenue cannot be recognized because control has not transferred under a
      valid contract. The lack of enforceable payment terms and cancellation
      provisions means that the entity has not satisfied the criteria for
      revenue recognition at a point in time or over time (ASC 606-10-25-1, ASC
      606-10-25-1).

      Facts and circumstances:  payment or cancellation terms left to be agreed; contract_valid=FALSE

      payment and cancellation terms remain to be agreed. As such, the contract
      does not meet the criteria for revenue recognition and must be escalated
      for further review.

   is reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 17 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   We have concluded that the contract with Driftwood Capital Partners, LLC
   is not valid under ASC 606 due to the absence of agreed payment and
   cancellation terms, which are essential for contract enforceability and
   revenue recognition. There is a single performance obligation to provide
   platform access, but the transaction price is zero for accounting
   purposes. Accordingly, no revenue should be recognized until the contract
   meets the validity criteria. This conclusion aligns with the relevant
   guidance and internal policies.

   Recognition status: Referred to technical accounting; recognition withheld

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Foxglove Wealth Advisors, LLC has entered into a 12-month contract with a
   total transaction price of $20,000, comprising a $15,000 annual platform
   subscription fee and a $5,000 onboarding fee. The contract includes
   standard updates and support as part of the subscription, with no usage-
   based fees or discounts. The contract is valid and enforceable, with
   defined parties, payment terms, and termination provisions. The onboarding
   service is required but not confirmed as standardized, optional, or
   required for platform access, and the contract does not specify these
   characteristics.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract
   ASC 606-10-25-21              Separately identifiable (factors)
   ASC 606-10-25-27              Recognition over time
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-55-50 to 55-51     Nonrefundable upfront fees
   ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-21; 55-51  Onboarding — non-distinct by default
   ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-22     Onboarding — distinct (exception)

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists under ASC 606 as the
      agreement has defined parties, an effective date, specified fees, payment
      terms payable in advance within 30 days, termination rights for breach
      with notice, and auto-renewal terms. There are no indications of
      collectibility issues or at-will cancellation without compensation.

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, onboarding is not distinct and is combined into the
      subscription.
      We have concluded that the contract contains a single performance
      obligation. Although there is a separately priced onboarding fee, the
      onboarding service is not distinct because it is not confirmed to be
      standardized, optional, or available from other vendors, and it is
      combined with the subscription service. Standard updates and support are
      part of the subscription and are not distinct performance obligations.

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $20,000.00. The arrangement includes no variable
      consideration and no discount.
      We have concluded that the transaction price is $20,000, which is the sum
      of the fixed fees for the 12-month term: $15,000 for the platform
      subscription and $5,000 for onboarding. There are no variable
      consideration components, discounts, or material rights affecting the
      transaction price.

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      We have concluded that the entire transaction price of $20,000 is
      allocated to the single combined performance obligation, as onboarding is
      not distinct from the subscription service.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.
      We have concluded that revenue should be recognised over time on a ratable
      basis over the 12-month contract term, consistent with the subscription
      service delivery. There is no usage-based pricing or other factors
      requiring a different recognition pattern.

   [CRITICAL] Undetermined distinctness or po count
      Facts and circumstances:  onboarding present but the contract does not state whether it is standardized, optional, or required for platform access
      Question:                 Are the facts sufficient to apply the distinct criteria and the separability factors? If not, the PO count should not be fixed; the specific missing facts (customization, dependency, third-party availability) should be flagged as open questions.
      Recommended procedure:    Obtain the missing facts on customisation, dependency and third-party availability before fixing the performance obligation count.
      Timing:                   Before any revenue is recognised
      Disposition:              open
      The contract includes onboarding, but it is undetermined whether
      onboarding is standardized, optional, or required for platform access.
      This missing information is critical to finalising the performance
      obligation count. It is necessary to obtain facts on customisation,
      dependency, and third-party availability before concluding the number of
      performance obligations.

   [HIGH] Bundled onboarding distinctness
      Facts and circumstances:  separately priced onboarding fee of 5000.0 alongside subscription of 15000.0
      Question:                 Is the onboarding customized to the customer and required before the customer can access the platform (suggesting it is not distinct and should be combined), or is it standardized, optional, and available elsewhere (suggesting a separate performance obligation)? Confirm whether the onboarding fee is being front-loaded rather than recognized over the service period.
      Recommended procedure:    Document whether the onboarding is customised and required for platform access (combine into the subscription obligation) or standardised and optional (separate obligation). Retain the evidence relied on.
      Timing:                   Before revenue is recognised
      Disposition:              open
      There is a separately priced onboarding fee of $5,000 alongside a $15,000
      subscription fee. It is important to document whether onboarding is
      customised and required for platform access, which would support combining
      it into the subscription obligation, or if it is standardized and
      optional, which would support treating it as a separate performance
      obligation. Retaining evidence supporting this conclusion is required.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Foxglove Wealth Advisors, LLC represents a
   valid enforceable agreement with a single combined performance obligation
   consisting of the platform subscription and onboarding services. The
   transaction price of $20,000 is fixed and allocated entirely to this
   performance obligation. Revenue is recognised ratably over the 12-month
   term, reflecting the continuous transfer of control of the subscription
   service. The onboarding service is not distinct and is combined with the
   subscription, consistent with ASC 606 guidance and RevLens policy. No
   variable consideration, discounts, or material rights are present. The
   matter is escalated due to the critical need to obtain additional facts
   regarding the onboarding service's characteristics to confirm the
   performance obligation count definitively.

Recognition status:  Not cleared for recognition pending resolution of the open items below
==============================================================================

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Cascade Point Advisors, LLC has entered into a contract for a subscription
   service with a fixed annual base fee of $12,000 and additional usage fees
   for transactions exceeding an included monthly allotment. The contract
   term is 12 months, with payment due annually in advance. The contract is
   valid, signed by both parties, and includes standard updates and support
   as part of the subscription. There are no separately priced onboarding
   fees, discounts, or material rights associated with renewal pricing. Usage
   fees are variable and apply above the included allotment without a cap or
   floor.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract; Contract validity and collectibility
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Cascade Point
      Advisors, LLC, as it is signed by both parties, includes defined payment
      terms, termination provisions, and there are no indications of
      collectibility issues. The contract meets the criteria for enforceability
      under ASC 606 and relevant RevLens policies (ASC 606-10-25-1).

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, there is no separately priced onboarding.
      The contract contains a single performance obligation consisting of
      providing the subscription service, which includes standard updates and
      support that are not distinct from the subscription itself (ASC
      606-10-25-14(b); 25-27). There is no separately priced onboarding or
      implementation service, and no additional distinct services are
      identified.

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $12,000.00. The arrangement includes variable
      consideration (base_plus_overage) and no discount.
      The transaction price is $12,000, representing the fixed annual base
      subscription fee for the 12-month term. Usage fees are excluded from the
      transaction price as they are variable consideration related to usage
      above the included allotment and are not fixed or determinable at contract
      inception (ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45, ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45). No
      discounts or material rights are present to adjust the transaction price.

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      The entire transaction price of $12,000 is allocated to the single
      performance obligation, as there are no other distinct goods or services
      to allocate to. The variable usage fees are accounted for separately and
      are not included in the fixed transaction price allocation.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.
      Revenue from the fixed base subscription fee is recognised ratably over
      the 12-month contract term, reflecting the continuous transfer of the
      subscription service over time. Variable consideration related to usage
      fees is recognised as the usage occurs, constrained to the amount highly
      probable not to reverse, consistent with the guidance for variable
      consideration in usage-based pricing models (Variable consideration
      present, Unestimable or volatile usage).

      Dr  1200 Accounts Receivable                         $12,000.00
          Cr  2400 Deferred Revenue - Current              $12,000.00

          Cr  4100 Subscription Revenue                     $1,000.00
      Release the contract liability as the subscription is delivered.

   As usage is incurred
      Dr  1200 Accounts Receivable                        as incurred
          Cr  4300 Usage Revenue                          as incurred
      Usage-based consideration (base_plus_overage) is variable and is recognised as the usage occurs.

7. OPEN ITEMS AND MATTERS FOR FOLLOW-UP

   [HIGH] Unestimable or volatile usage
      Facts and circumstances:  usage with no fixed fee=False, volatile or seasonal=False, no basis for estimation=True
      Question:                 Has the variable consideration estimate been constrained to the amount highly probable not to reverse? Where usage is seasonal or unpredictable, is revenue recognized only as the usage occurs rather than estimated for the full term?
      Recommended procedure:    Constrain the variable consideration estimate to the amount highly probable not to reverse; recognise as usage occurs where volume is seasonal or unpredictable.
      Timing:                   Before revenue is recognised
      Disposition:              open
      The usage fees represent variable consideration with no cap or floor and
      no reliable basis for estimation due to seasonal or unpredictable volume.
      Accordingly, the variable consideration estimate should be constrained to
      the amount highly probable not to reverse, and revenue should be
      recognised as usage occurs.

   [MEDIUM] Variable consideration present
      Facts and circumstances:  usage-based pricing present; usage_model=base_plus_overage
      Question:                 Has the variable portion been identified and separated from any fixed fee? Is RevLens recognizing usage as it is invoiced/incurred (right-to-invoice), or does the arrangement require estimating variable consideration? Confirm the fixed portion's recognition is not inflated by unearned usage.
      Recommended procedure:    Confirm the variable portion is separated from the fixed fee and is recognised as usage occurs rather than estimated for the full term.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      Given the presence of usage-based pricing under a base plus overage model,
      it is important to confirm that the variable portion is separated from the
      fixed fee and recognised as usage occurs rather than being estimated for
      the full term.

8. MATTERS CONSIDERED AND CONCLUDED IMMATERIAL

   No quantified matter was concluded immaterial on this contract.

9. COMPLETENESS OF REVIEW

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 2
   are reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 16 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Cascade Point Advisors, LLC is valid and
   contains a single performance obligation for the subscription service. The
   transaction price of $12,000 represents the fixed annual base fee, which
   is recognised ratably over the contract term. Variable usage fees are
   excluded from the fixed transaction price and are recognised as incurred,
   constrained to amounts not expected to reverse. There are no discounts,
   material rights, or separately priced onboarding services affecting the
   revenue recognition. This accounting treatment aligns with ASC 606 and
   relevant RevLens policies.

   Recognition status: Not cleared for recognition pending resolution of the open items below

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

   [HIGH] Silent renewal materiality
      Facts and circumstances:  a renewal option exists but the contract does not state its price or how it compares to standard pricing
      Question:                 Is the renewal price specified, and is it at, above, or below the price offered to comparable customers? Without this, whether the option conveys a material right cannot be concluded and should be flagged.
      Recommended procedure:    Obtain the renewal price and how it compares to standard pricing for comparable customers before concluding on the material right.
      Timing:                   Before revenue is recognised
      Disposition:              open
      A renewal option exists in the contract, but the contract does not specify
      the renewal price or how it compares to standard pricing. Per Silent
      renewal materiality, it is necessary to obtain the renewal price and its
      comparison to standard pricing for comparable customers before concluding
      whether a material right exists. This information is critical to
      finalizing the revenue recognition assessment.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Birchmere Capital, LLC is valid and contains
   a single performance obligation for a fixed annual subscription fee of
   $20,000. The transaction price is fixed and fully allocated to this
   subscription service. However, the presence of a renewal option with an

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Harborview Investment Management, LLC has entered into a 12-month contract
   for a platform subscription service with a total transaction price of
   $30,000. The contract includes a fixed base subscription fee of $24,000
   annually and a one-time implementation service fee of $6,000. The contract
   also incorporates a usage-based pricing model with overage fees above an
   included allotment, without any cap or floor. The contract is valid with
   defined parties, effective date, payment terms, and termination
   provisions. There are no discounts or material rights embedded in the
   contract, and no additional distinct services beyond the subscription and
   implementation are identified.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-21; 55-51  Onboarding — non-distinct by default
   ASC 606-10-32-5; 55-18        Usage/overage — as incurred (right-to-invoice)
   ASC 606-10-32-36 to 32-37     Discount allocation — pro-rata default vs directed

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Harborview Investment
      Management, LLC, as the contract has defined parties, an effective date,
      fixed fees with payment terms payable in advance within 30 days,
      termination provisions for breach, and probable collection, consistent
      with ASC 606 and RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-05-4).

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, onboarding is not distinct and is combined into the
      subscription.
      The contract contains a single performance obligation comprising the
      platform subscription service, which includes the implementation services.
      The implementation is not distinct because production access to the
      platform is conditioned on completion of implementation, and the
      implementation is combined with the subscription service into one
      performance obligation (ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27). No other distinct
      services or material rights are present, as the contract auto-renews at
      list pricing without a renewal price below standard pricing (ASC
      606-10-55-42 to 55-45, ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45).

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $30,000.00. The arrangement includes variable
      consideration (base_plus_overage) and no discount.
      The transaction price is $30,000, consisting of a $24,000 annual base
      subscription fee and a $6,000 one-time implementation fee. The contract
      includes a usage-based pricing model with overage fees above an included
      allotment, which is accounted for as variable consideration within the
      subscription performance obligation and recognized as invoiced, consistent
      with RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-32-5; 55-18). There are no discounts or
      reductions to the transaction price below standalone selling prices.

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      The entire transaction price of $30,000 is allocated to the single
      combined performance obligation, as the implementation service is not
      distinct from the subscription service. The allocation reflects the sum of
      the standalone selling prices of the base subscription and implementation
      services, which together comprise the total contract price.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 12 months.
      Revenue is recognized over time on a ratable basis over the 12-month
      contract term, reflecting the continuous transfer of control of the
      subscription service to the customer. The variable consideration related
      to usage overage fees is recognized as invoiced, consistent with the usage
      model of base plus overage. No contract assets or liabilities arise beyond
      standard invoicing and payment terms.

7. OPEN ITEMS AND MATTERS FOR FOLLOW-UP

   [MEDIUM] Bundled onboarding distinctness
      Facts and circumstances:  separately priced onboarding fee of 6000.0 alongside subscription of 24000.0
      Question:                 Is the onboarding customized to the customer and required before the customer can access the platform (suggesting it is not distinct and should be combined), or is it standardized, optional, and available elsewhere (suggesting a separate performance obligation)? Confirm whether the onboarding fee is being front-loaded rather than recognized over the service period.
      Recommended procedure:    Document whether the onboarding is customised and required for platform access (combine into the subscription obligation) or standardised and optional (separate obligation). Retain the evidence relied on.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      There is a separately priced onboarding fee of $6,000 alongside the
      $24,000 subscription fee. It is necessary to document whether the
      onboarding is customized and required for platform access, which would
      support combining it into the subscription performance obligation, or if
      it is standardized and optional, which would support treating it as a
      separate performance obligation. The evidence relied upon for this
      conclusion should be retained.

   [MEDIUM] Variable consideration present
      Facts and circumstances:  usage-based pricing present; usage_model=base_plus_overage
      Question:                 Has the variable portion been identified and separated from any fixed fee? Is RevLens recognizing usage as it is invoiced/incurred (right-to-invoice), or does the arrangement require estimating variable consideration? Confirm the fixed portion's recognition is not inflated by unearned usage.
      Recommended procedure:    Confirm the variable portion is separated from the fixed fee and is recognised as usage occurs rather than estimated for the full term.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The contract includes usage-based pricing with a base plus overage model.
      It is important to confirm that the variable portion of the transaction
      price is separated from the fixed fee and recognized as usage occurs
      rather than being estimated for the full term, consistent with the
      accounting policy.

   No quantified matter was concluded immaterial on this contract.

   are reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 16 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Harborview Investment Management, LLC
   represents a single performance obligation combining the platform
   subscription and implementation services. The transaction price of $30,000
   is allocated entirely to this combined obligation. Revenue is recognized
   ratably over the 12-month term, with variable usage fees recognized as
   invoiced. The contract is valid and does not contain discounts or material
   rights that would affect revenue recognition. This accounting treatment
   aligns with ASC 606 and relevant RevLens policies.

   Recognition status: Cleared for recognition; open items to be resolved before period close

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable

   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Northwind Investment Group, LLC entered into a 12-month contract with
   RevLens for platform access, onboarding services, and usage-based
   transactions. The total transaction price is $41,000, comprising a $36,000
   annual subscription minimum commitment and a $5,000 one-time onboarding
   fee. The contract includes a fixed minimum subscription fee plus usage
   fees above an included monthly allotment at a per-transaction rate with no
   cap or floor. The contract auto-renews at then-current list pricing and
   contains standard updates and support as part of the subscription. There
   are no discounts or material rights embedded in the contract. The contract
   is valid with enforceable rights and probable collection.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Customer options / material right; Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-55-50 to 55-51     Nonrefundable upfront fees
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-22     Onboarding — distinct (exception)
   ASC 606-10-32-5; 55-18        Usage/overage — as incurred (right-to-invoice)

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Northwind Investment
      Group, LLC, as it has a defined effective date, subscription term, fees,
      payment terms, termination provisions, and no collectibility concerns,
      consistent with ASC 606 requirements (ASC 606-10-25-1).

   Identify the performance obligations
      2 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription,
      onboarding(distinct). On onboarding, the onboarding is a distinct
      performance obligation.
      The contract contains two performance obligations. The onboarding service
      is distinct because platform access does not depend on RevLens performing
      the implementation, satisfying the distinct-service exception; therefore,
      onboarding is a separate performance obligation. The subscription service,
      which includes platform access, standard updates, and support, constitutes
      the second performance obligation. Usage-based transactions are considered
      part of the subscription performance obligation under a minimum commitment
      usage model.

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $41,000.00. The arrangement includes variable
      consideration (minimum_commitment) and no discount.
      The transaction price is $41,000, consisting of a $36,000 fixed annual
      subscription minimum commitment and a $5,000 one-time onboarding fee.
      There are no discounts or material rights affecting the transaction price,
      as the contract does not provide renewal pricing below list or any other
      discount terms (ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45, ASC 606-10-55-50 to 55-51, ASC
      606-10-55-42 to 55-45, ASC 606-10-55-50 to 55-51). Variable consideration
      arises from usage fees above the included allotment, which are recognized
      as incurred per RevLens policy (ASC 606-10-32-5; 55-18).

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      Allocation across performance obligations by relative standalone selling
      price (SSP) is NOT implemented in this analysis; a multi-obligation
      schedule must be reallocated before use.
      The transaction price is allocated to the two performance obligations
      based on their standalone selling prices. The onboarding fee of $5,000 is
      separately priced and allocated entirely to the onboarding performance
      obligation. The remaining $36,000 corresponds to the subscription minimum
      commitment, allocated to the subscription performance obligation, which
      includes usage-based fees recognized as variable consideration when
      incurred.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised the subscription ratably over time and the
      onboarding at a point in time over 12 months.
      Revenue for the onboarding performance obligation is recognized at a point
      in time upon completion of the onboarding service. Revenue related to the
      subscription minimum commitment is recognized ratably over the 12-month
      contract term. Usage fees above the included allotment are recognized as
      incurred, consistent with the minimum commitment usage model.

   [MEDIUM] Bundled onboarding distinctness

Confirm whether the onboarding fee is being front-loaded rather than recognized over the service period.

      subscription fee requires documentation to confirm whether onboarding is
      customized and required for platform access, which would combine it into
      the subscription obligation, or standardized and optional, supporting its
      classification as a separate performance obligation. Evidence supporting
      this conclusion should be retained.

      Facts and circumstances:  usage-based pricing present; usage_model=minimum_commitment
      Question:                 Has the variable portion been identified and separated from any fixed fee? Is RevLens recognizing usage as it is invoiced/incurred (right-to-invoice), or does the arrangement require estimating variable consideration?       Timing:                   Before period close

      model, it is necessary to confirm that the variable portion of
      consideration is separated from the fixed fee and recognized as usage
      occurs rather than estimated for the full term.

   [MEDIUM] Minimum commitment floor
      Facts and circumstances:  a stated minimum or committed fee alongside usage above an included volume
      Question:                 Is the committed minimum being recognized as fixed consideration over the term, with only usage above the included volume treated as variable? Confirm the minimum is recognized regardless of actual usage.
      Recommended procedure:    Confirm the committed minimum is recognised as fixed consideration over the term, with only usage above the included volume treated as variable.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The contract includes a stated minimum or committed fee alongside usage
      above an included volume. It should be confirmed that the committed
      minimum is recognized as fixed consideration over the term, with only
      usage above the included volume treated as variable consideration.

8. MATTERS CONSIDERED AND CONCLUDED IMMATERIAL

   Proration partial period
      The item was triggered on the facts. The exposure is $1,400.00 against a
      materiality threshold for this contract of $2,050.00 (5% of the
      transaction price, floor $1,000.00). We consider the amount immaterial to
      the financial statements taken as a whole and no adjustment has been
      recorded.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Northwind Investment Group, LLC is valid and
   contains two distinct performance obligations: onboarding and subscription
   services. The transaction price of $41,000 is allocated between these
   obligations based on their standalone selling prices. Revenue for
   onboarding is recognized at a point in time, while subscription revenue is
   recognized ratably over the contract term. Usage fees above the minimum
   commitment are variable consideration recognized as incurred. There are no
   discounts or material rights affecting the accounting. This treatment
   aligns with ASC 606 and RevLens policies.

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-18              Right-to-invoice practical expedient

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Castlerock Wealth
      Partners, LLC as it meets the criteria under ASC 606, including defined
      parties, an effective date, term, fees, payment terms, and termination
      rights, with no collectibility concerns.

   Identify the performance obligations
      1 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription. On
      onboarding, onboarding is not distinct and is combined into the
      subscription.
      The contract contains a single performance obligation comprising the
      subscription service combined with the implementation services. The

      fixed_prorata.
      The transaction price is $63,000, which includes a $60,000 subscription
      fee over two years, an $8,000 one-time implementation fee, and a $5,000
      discount applied to the bundle. Usage fees are variable and excluded from
      the fixed transaction price.

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      A single performance obligation was identified; no allocation is required.
      The transaction price is allocated entirely to the single combined
      performance obligation. The $5,000 discount is allocated on a fixed
      prorata basis across the subscription and implementation components, as
      there is no evidence that the discount relates solely to one component,
      consistent with ASC 606 and RevLens policy.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised ratably over time over 24 months.
      Revenue is recognized over time on a ratable basis over the 24-month
      contract term, reflecting the continuous transfer of control of the
      subscription service. Usage-based royalty fees are recognized as invoiced,
      consistent with the usage_royalty model.

7. OPEN ITEMS AND MATTERS FOR FOLLOW-UP

   [MEDIUM] Bundled onboarding distinctness
      Facts and circumstances:  separately priced onboarding fee of 8000.0 alongside subscription of 60000.0
      Question:                 Is the onboarding customized to the customer and required before the customer can access the platform (suggesting it is not distinct and should be combined), or is it standardized, optional, and available elsewhere (suggesting a separate performance obligation)? Confirm whether the onboarding fee is being front-loaded rather than recognized over the service period.
      Recommended procedure:    Document whether the onboarding is customised and required for platform access (combine into the subscription obligation) or standardised and optional (separate obligation). Retain the evidence relied on.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The onboarding fee of $8,000 is combined with the subscription performance
      obligation because the implementation services are customized and required
      for platform access, consistent with RevLens policy. Documentation should
      confirm this characterization and the evidence relied upon.

   [MEDIUM] Usage royalty vs ordinary usage
      Facts and circumstances:  royalty or licensed-IP language=True; purchasable capacity blocks=False
      Question:                 Is the variable fee consideration for a license of RevLens intellectual property (apply the royalty exception), an ordinary metered usage fee (variable consideration), or the purchase of new capacity the customer did not previously control (a separate option/purchase)? Confirm the correct one.
      Recommended procedure:    Confirm whether the variable fee is a licence royalty, ordinary metered usage, or the purchase of new capacity, and apply the corresponding model.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The variable fee is characterized as a royalty for licensed intellectual
      property rather than ordinary metered usage or the purchase of new
      capacity. Confirmation of this classification is necessary to apply the
      appropriate revenue recognition model.

   [MEDIUM] Discount allocation method
      Facts and circumstances:  combined standalone price None exceeds the bundled total 63000.0; discount_type=fixed_prorata
      Question:                 Is the discount allocated proportionately across all performance obligations by default? A discount is directed to a specific obligation only where RevLens regularly sells each item separately and regularly sells the discounted item on its own at a comparable discount — is that evidence present?
      Recommended procedure:    Obtain the standalone selling price of each component and allocate the discount proportionately unless the directed-discount evidence is present.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The $5,000 discount is allocated on a fixed prorata basis across the
      subscription and implementation components due to the absence of evidence
      directing the discount to a specific component. Standalone selling prices
      should be obtained to support this allocation.

   No quantified matter was concluded immaterial on this contract.

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 3

   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed

   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Castlerock Wealth Partners, LLC is valid and
   contains a single combined performance obligation consisting of
   subscription and implementation services. The transaction price of
   $63,000, net of a $5,000 discount, is allocated to this obligation.
   Revenue is recognized ratably over the 24-month term, with variable usage-
   based royalty fees recognized as invoiced. There are no material rights or
   additional distinct services requiring separate accounting treatment.

   Recognition status: Cleared for recognition; open items to be resolved before period close

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

Contract:            C49
Customer:            Everwood Asset Management, LLC
Prepared by:         Treatment Agent (Agent 3)
Reviewed by:         Audit Agent (Agent 4) - 18-item review checklist
Standard:            ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with Customers
Recognition status:  Not cleared for recognition pending resolution of the open items below
==============================================================================

1. PURPOSE AND SCOPE

   This memorandum documents the revenue recognition analysis for the
   arrangement identified above under ASC 606, Revenue from Contracts with
   Customers, and records the accounting conclusion, the recognition
   schedule, the journal entries, and the open items for follow-up.

2. FACTS AND CIRCUMSTANCES

   Everwood Asset Management, LLC entered into a 12-month contract with
   RevLens for platform subscription services, onboarding, and usage-based
   fees. The total transaction price is $49,000, comprising a $42,000 annual
   subscription fee and a $7,000 one-time onboarding fee. The contract
   includes a renewal option at a discounted price of $21,000 compared to the
   standard $42,000 renewal price for comparable customers. Usage fees are
   charged at $0.05 per transaction above a base allotment, capped at $5,000
   over the term. The contract is valid with committed parties, identifiable
   rights, payment terms, and probable collection. Standard updates and
   support are included but are not distinct services.

3. AUTHORITATIVE GUIDANCE

   ASC 606-10-05-4               Revenue model — overview
   ASC 606-10-55-18              Right-to-invoice practical expedient
   ASC 606-10-25-1               Identifying the contract
   ASC 606-10-25-14(b); 25-27    Subscription — single PO, ratable
   ASC 606-10-55-42 to 55-45     Material rights — discounted renewals/options
   ASC 606-10-25-19 to 25-22     Onboarding — distinct (exception)

   The paragraph references above point to the relevant provisions of ASC
   606; the entity's revenue policy paraphrases those provisions and should
   be read together with them.

4. ANALYSIS

   Identify the contract with the customer
      The Company has concluded that a valid, enforceable contract exists.
      We have concluded that a valid contract exists with Everwood Asset
      Management, LLC, as the contract meets ASC 606 criteria including
      committed parties, identifiable rights, payment terms, and probable
      collection, supported by fixed fees payable within 30 days and termination
      provisions.

   Identify the performance obligations
      3 performance obligation(s) were identified: core_subscription,
      onboarding(distinct), material_right(renewal). On onboarding, the
      onboarding is a distinct performance obligation.
      The contract contains three distinct performance obligations: the annual
      platform subscription, the onboarding engagement, and the usage-based
      services. The onboarding is distinct because platform access does not
      depend on RevLens performing the implementation, satisfying the distinct-
      service exception. Standard updates and support are not distinct and are
      included in the subscription obligation.

   Determine the transaction price
      The transaction price is $49,000.00. The arrangement includes variable
      consideration (base_plus_capped) and a discount characterised as
      renewal_option.
      The transaction price is $49,000, consisting of a fixed annual
      subscription fee of $42,000 and a one-time onboarding fee of $7,000. Usage
      fees and renewal prices are excluded from the transaction price
      calculation. The usage-based fees follow a base plus capped model with a
      $0.05 per transaction overage capped at $5,000 for the term. The contract
      also includes a renewal option priced at $21,000, which is a significant
      discount compared to the standard $42,000 renewal price, constituting a
      material right.

   Allocate the transaction price to the performance obligations
      Allocation across performance obligations by relative standalone selling
      price (SSP) is NOT implemented in this analysis; a multi-obligation
      schedule must be reallocated before use.
      The transaction price is allocated to the three performance obligations
      based on their relative standalone selling prices. The onboarding fee is
      separately priced at $7,000, and the subscription fee is $42,000. The
      material right related to the discounted renewal option requires separate
      consideration in allocation as per RevLens policy and ASC 606 guidance.

   Recognise revenue as performance obligations are satisfied
      Revenue is recognised the subscription ratably over time and the
      onboarding at a point in time over 12 months.
      Revenue for the subscription is recognised ratably over the 12-month term,
      reflecting the continuous transfer of control. The onboarding revenue is
      recognised at a point in time upon completion of the onboarding service.
      Usage-based fees are recognised as incurred, consistent with the base plus
      capped usage model.

7. OPEN ITEMS AND MATTERS FOR FOLLOW-UP

   [HIGH] Material right renewal or fee
      Facts and circumstances:  nonrefundable upfront fee=False; discounted renewal option=True; material_right=TRUE
      Question:                 Does the renewal/option price sit significantly below the price offered to comparable customers (a material right), requiring a portion of the transaction price to be allocated to it and recognized at exercise or expiry? For an upfront fee waived on renewal, has the material-right question been assessed?
      Recommended procedure:    Obtain the renewal price offered to comparable customers and assess whether the option conveys a material right requiring separate allocation.
      Timing:                   Before revenue is recognised
      Disposition:              open
      The renewal option is priced at $21,000, significantly below the standard
      $42,000 renewal price for comparable customers, indicating a material
      right. It is necessary to obtain and document the renewal prices offered
      to comparable customers to confirm that the option conveys a material
      right requiring separate allocation.

   [MEDIUM] Bundled onboarding distinctness
      Facts and circumstances:  separately priced onboarding fee of 7000.0 alongside subscription of 42000.0
      Question:                 Is the onboarding customized to the customer and required before the customer can access the platform (suggesting it is not distinct and should be combined), or is it standardized, optional, and available elsewhere (suggesting a separate performance obligation)? Confirm whether the onboarding fee is being front-loaded rather than recognized over the service period.
      Recommended procedure:    Document whether the onboarding is customised and required for platform access (combine into the subscription obligation) or standardised and optional (separate obligation). Retain the evidence relied on.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The onboarding fee of $7,000 is separately priced alongside the $42,000
      subscription fee. Documentation should confirm whether the onboarding is
      standardised and optional, supporting its treatment as a separate
      performance obligation, or if it is customised and required for platform
      access, which would require combining it with the subscription obligation.

   [MEDIUM] Variable consideration present
      Facts and circumstances:  usage-based pricing present; usage_model=base_plus_capped
      Question:                 Has the variable portion been identified and separated from any fixed fee? Is RevLens recognizing usage as it is invoiced/incurred (right-to-invoice), or does the arrangement require estimating variable consideration? Confirm the fixed portion's recognition is not inflated by unearned usage.
      Recommended procedure:    Confirm the variable portion is separated from the fixed fee and is recognised as usage occurs rather than estimated for the full term.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      The contract includes usage-based pricing following a base plus capped
      model. It is important to confirm that the variable consideration is
      separated from the fixed fee and recognised as usage occurs rather than
      being estimated for the full term.

   [MEDIUM] Multiple performance obligations
      Facts and circumstances:  num_POs=3; recognition_method=sub:over_time_ratable; onboarding:point_in_time; usage:as_incurred
      Question:                 Has the transaction price been allocated across all obligations by relative SSP, and is each obligation recognized on its correct pattern (over time for the subscription; at completion for distinct one-time deliverables; by effort/hours for professional services)?
      Recommended procedure:    Allocate the transaction price across all obligations by relative standalone selling price and confirm each is recognised on its own pattern.
      Timing:                   Before period close
      Disposition:              open
      With three performance obligations and differing recognition
      methods—subscription over time ratably, onboarding at a point in time, and
      usage as incurred—the transaction price allocation should be based on
      relative standalone selling prices, and each obligation should be
      recognised according to its specific pattern.

8. MATTERS CONSIDERED AND CONCLUDED IMMATERIAL

   No quantified matter was concluded immaterial on this contract.

9. COMPLETENESS OF REVIEW

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 4
   are reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 14 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

10. CONCLUSION

   In summary, the contract with Everwood Asset Management, LLC is valid and
   contains three distinct performance obligations: subscription, onboarding,
   and usage-based services. The transaction price of $49,000 is allocated
   based on standalone selling prices, with the onboarding fee treated as a
   separate obligation. The renewal option at a discounted price constitutes
   a material right requiring separate allocation. Revenue is recognised over
   time for the subscription, at a point in time for onboarding, and as
   incurred for usage fees, consistent with ASC 606 requirements and RevLens
   policies.

   Recognition status: Not cleared for recognition pending resolution of the open items below

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across
   performance obligations by relative standalone selling price is not
   implemented; partial periods are not prorated by days; variable
   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
   incurred.

   The 18-item review checklist was completed in full. Of the 18 matters, 5
   are reported at section 7, 0 at section 8, and 13 were considered and
   concluded not applicable to this contract on the facts. The completed
   checklist, with the basis for each conclusion, is retained in the
   structured review record.

   We have concluded that the transaction price of $22,000.00 is recognised
   ratably over time over 12 months across 1 performance obligation(s).

   Recognition status: Not cleared for recognition pending resolution of the open items below

   Scope and limitations: allocation of the transaction price across

   consideration is excluded from the transaction price and recognised as
